{\mathsf{F}}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})=\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{e^{\mathsf{c}_{-}^{2}(t_{i}-t_{i-1})u_{i}^{2}+[-(x_{i}-x_{i-1})+(h_{i}-h_{i-1})]u_{i}}}{e^{-\mathsf{c}_{+}^{2}(t_{i}-t_{i-1})v_{i}^{2}-[(x_{i}-x_{i-1})+(h_{i}-h_{i-1})]v_{i}}}.

(6.22)

Because the function
{\mathsf{F}}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})
decays super-exponentially fast in each variable as it tends to infinity in any closed sector strictly contained in
\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:\arg(z)\in(\frac{\pi}{4},\frac{3\pi}{4})\cup(-\frac{3\pi}{4},-\frac{\pi}{4})\}
, extending the contours
\vec{\Sigma}^{-}_{L}
and
\vec{\Sigma}^{+}_{L}
, and applying the dominated convergence theorem, we find that

\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\sqrt{D}}{\ell(\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}L)^{m-1}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\int_{\vec{\Sigma}^{-}_{L}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\int_{\vec{\Sigma}^{+}_{L}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}\,\hat{\Pi}_{\mathbf{1}}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})\hat{\mathsf{F}}_{L}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})

converges, as
L\to\infty
, to

(-1)^{m-1}\int_{\vec{\Sigma}^{-}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{u}\int_{\vec{\Sigma}^{+}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}\,\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\frac{1}{(u_{i+1}-u_{i})(v_{i+1}-v_{i})}\right]{\mathsf{F}}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})

(6.23)

where
\vec{\Sigma}^{-}=\Sigma_{1}^{-}\times\cdots\times\Sigma_{m}^{-}
and

\vec{\Sigma}^{+}=\Sigma_{1}^{+}\times\cdots\times\Sigma_{m}^{+}
,
with
\Sigma_{i}^{\pm}=i+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}
for
1\leq i\leq m
.
All contours
\Sigma_{i}^{\pm}
are oriented upwards.

From (
6.18
), (
6.21
), and (
6.23
), we conclude that

\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{4\pi\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathsf{c}_{-}\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}L\sqrt{D}}{\ell\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{1})}_{m}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})=\mathsf{P}_{1}\mathsf{P}_{2}

where

\mathsf{P}_{1}:=\frac{\sqrt{4\pi}\mathsf{c}_{-}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{m}}\int_{\vec{\Sigma}^{-}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{m}e^{\mathsf{c}_{-}^{2}(t_{i}-t_{i-1})u_{i}^{2}+[-(x_{i}-x_{i-1})+(h_{i}-h_{i-1})]u_{i}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(u_{i+1}-u_{i})}\text{d}\mathbf{u}

and

\mathsf{P}_{2}:=\frac{\sqrt{4\pi}\mathsf{c}_{+}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{m}}\int_{\vec{\Sigma}^{+}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{m}e^{\mathsf{c}_{+}^{2}(t_{i}-t_{i-1})v_{i}^{2}+[(x_{i}-x_{i-1})+(h_{i}-h_{i-1})]v_{i}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(v_{i+1}-v_{i})}\text{d}\mathbf{v}.

By Lemma
4.1
,

\mathsf{P}_{1}=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{m-1}\left\{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{c}_{-}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}_{-}(t_{i})>-x_{i}+h_{i}\right\}\right),\qquad\mathsf{P}_{2}=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^{m-1}\left\{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}_{+}(t_{i})>x_{i}+h_{i}\right\}\right)

for independent Brownian Bridges
\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}_{+}
and
\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}_{-}
.
Noting
\frac{4\pi\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathsf{c}_{-}\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}L\sqrt{D}}{\ell\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=\frac{2\pi LD}{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}
, we obtain Lemma
6.1
.

6.3
Proof of Lemma
6.2

We take the
z_{i}
-contours in the formula (
3.11
) of
\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{m}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})
to be circles of fixed radii greater than
1
. For concreteness, we set them to be the circles of radii
2
centered at the origin. Then,

\left|\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{m}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})\right|\leq 3^{|\mathbf{n}|}\max_{|z_{i}|=2,\,i=1,\cdots,m-1}\left|\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\mathbf{z})\right|.

(6.24)

Consider now the formula (
3.8
) for
\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M},\mathbf{N},\mathbf{T}}(\mathbf{z})
.
Recall the circles
\Sigma^{b,L}_{\pm}
in Lemma
5.3
.
We take the contours as

C_{1,\textnormal{left}}=\Sigma^{0,L}_{-}
,
C_{1,\textnormal{right}}=\Sigma^{0,L}_{+}
, and,
for
i=2,\cdots,m
,

C_{i,\textnormal{left}}^{\textnormal{in}}=\Sigma^{-(i-1),L}_{-},\quad C_{i,\textnormal{left}}^{\textnormal{out}}=\Sigma^{i-1,L}_{-},\quad C_{i,\textnormal{right}}^{\textnormal{in}}=\Sigma^{-(i-1),L}_{+},\quad C_{i,\textnormal{right}}^{\textnormal{out}}=\Sigma^{i-1,L}_{+}.

Since the lengths of all contours are at most
2\pi
and
|z_{i}|=2
, we find that

\left|\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\mathbf{z})\right|\leq 3^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\max_{(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\in\vec{C}_{\textnormal{left}}\times\vec{C}_{\textnormal{right}}}|\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})||{\mathsf{F}}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})|,

(6.25)

where we set

\vec{C}_{\textnormal{left}}=\left(C_{1,\textnormal{left}}\right)^{n_{1}}\times\left(C^{\textnormal{in}}_{2,\textnormal{left}}\cup C^{\textnormal{out}}_{2,\textnormal{left}}\right)^{n_{2}}\times\cdots\times\left(C^{\textnormal{in}}_{m,\textnormal{left}}\cup C^{\textnormal{out}}_{m,\textnormal{left}}\right)^{n_{m}},

and

\vec{C}_{\textnormal{right}}=\left(C_{1,\textnormal{right}}\right)^{n_{1}}\times\left(C^{\textnormal{in}}_{2,\textnormal{right}}\cup C^{\textnormal{out}}_{2,\textnormal{right}}\right)^{n_{2}}\times\cdots\times\left(C^{\textnormal{in}}_{m,\textnormal{right}}\cup C^{\textnormal{out}}_{m,\textnormal{right}}\right)^{n_{m}}.

Consider the term
\left|\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|
given in (
3.5
).
By Hadamard’s inequality,

|\mathrm{K}(\mathbf{w}|\mathbf{w}^{\prime})|=\left|\det\left(\frac{1}{w_{i}-w_{j}^{\prime}}\right)\right|\leq\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|w_{i}-w_{j}^{\prime}|^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\leq\frac{n^{n/2}}{d^{n}}

(6.26)

for every
\mathbf{w}=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{n})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}=(w_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,w_{n}^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
, provided that

\min_{i,j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}}|w_{i}-w_{j}^{\prime}|\geq d>0
.
Thus, for every
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\in\vec{C}_{\textnormal{left}}\times\vec{C}_{\textnormal{right}}
, using
d=L^{-1/2}
in (
6.26
),

\begin{split}\left|\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1}|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1})\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{i},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{i+1}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{i},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{i+1})\right]\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{m})\right|\leq n_{1}^{\frac{n_{1}}{2}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(n_{i}+n_{i+1})^{\frac{n_{i}+n_{i+1}}{2}}\right]n_{m}^{\frac{n_{m}}{2}}L^{|\mathbf{n}|}.\end{split}

Recall the basic bound of factorials:
n!\geq n^{n}e^{-n}
for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Thus,
n^{n}\leq e^{n}n!\leq 4^{n}n!
, and hence,
(a+b)^{a+b}\leq 4^{a+b}(a+b)!\leq 8^{a+b}a!b!
for every
a,b\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Therefore,

n_{1}^{\frac{n_{1}}{2}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}(n_{i}+n_{i+1})^{\frac{n_{i}+n_{i+1}}{2}}\right]n_{m}^{\frac{n_{m}}{2}}\leq\frac{8^{|\mathbf{n}|}}{2^{(n_{1}+n_{m})/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{m}n_{i}!=\frac{8^{|\mathbf{n}|}\mathbf{n}!}{2^{(n_{1}+n_{m})/2}}.

Now, for all large enough
L
, the contours
C^{\text{in/out}}_{\text{left/right}}
are contained a disk of radius
2
. Hence,

|\mathrm{S}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{m})|=\left|\sum_{k_{m}=1}^{n_{m}}(\xi^{m}_{L,k_{m}}-\eta^{m}_{L,k_{m}})\right|\leq 4n_{m}.

Thus,

\max_{(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\in\vec{C}_{\textnormal{left}}\times\vec{C}_{\textnormal{right}}}\left|\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|\leq 4n_{m}\frac{8^{|\mathbf{n}|}\mathbf{n}!}{2^{(n_{1}+n_{m})/2}}\leq 8^{|\mathbf{n}|+1}\mathbf{n}!

(6.27)

for all sufficiently large
L
.

By Lemma
5.3
and using (
6.15
), there exist constants
C>0
and
L_{0}>0
such that

\left|{\mathsf{F}}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|\leq C^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left|\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(z^{-})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(z^{+})}\right|^{n_{i}}=C^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\left|\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(z^{-})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(z^{+})}\right|^{n_{i}-1}

for every
L\geq L_{0}
and
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contours. Thus, by (
6.17
),

\frac{1}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\left|{\mathsf{F}}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|\leq C^{2|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-\frac{\tau}{2}(|\mathbf{n}|-m)\mathsf{J}(\ell)L}.

(6.28)

From (
6.24
), (
6.25
), (
6.27
), and (
6.28
), we find that there exist constants
C>0
and
L_{1}>0
such that

\frac{1}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\left|\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{m}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})\right|\leq C^{|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-\frac{\tau}{2}(|\mathbf{n}|-m)\mathsf{J}(\ell)L}\mathbf{n}!

for all
L\geq L_{1}
and
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}
.
Now, if
\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{1}
, then
|\mathbf{n}|\geq m+1
and thus
|\mathbf{n}|-m\geq\frac{1}{m+1}|\mathbf{n}|
. Hence,

\frac{1}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\left|\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{m}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})\right|\leq C^{|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-\frac{\tau\mathsf{J}(\ell)}{2(m+1)}|\mathbf{n}|L}\mathbf{n}!\qquad\text{for $\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}$.}

(6.29)

Therefore, there exist constants
c>0
and
L_{2}>0
such that

\frac{1}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}\}}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{n}!)^{2}}\left|\mathsf{Q}_{m}^{(\mathbf{n})}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})\right|\leq e^{-cL}

(6.30)

for all
L\geq L_{2}
. This proves Lemma
6.2
.

7
Proof of Theorem
1.5

In the proof of Theorem
1.4
, the leading terms in the exponent of the functions
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(z)
in (
6.9
) are given by the same function
\mathrm{G}(z)
from (
6.10
) for every
i=1,\cdots,m
. However, for Theorem
1.5
, the leading functions depend on
i
. This implies that each function has different critical points and thus requires different contours.
Because of the nesting structure of the original contours,
which may not be in a suitable order, and the form of the rational function
\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
, it becomes necessary to account for the poles. Keeping track of the residues coming from these poles introduces technical difficulties in proving Theorem
1.5
.
For these reasons, we prove Theorem
1.5
only for two-point distributions, leaving the problem of multi-point distribution convergence to future work.

Fix
a,b>0
and
\ell>\bar{\mathcal{L}}(a,b)
.
Let
(x_{1},y_{1})
and
(x_{2},y_{2})
be distinct points in the square
(0,1)^{2}
satisfying
\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1
or
1<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<\mathrm{m}
, where, recalling from (
1.12
),

\mathrm{m}=\frac{\ell-a-b+\sqrt{D}}{\ell-a-b-\sqrt{D}},\qquad D=\ell^{2}-2(a+b)\ell+(a-b)^{2}.

Since
\mathcal{L}(m,n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{L}(n,m)
, it suffices to consider one of these cases. Without loss of generality, we assume

\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1.

(7.1)

Recall the function
h(x,y)
from (
1.10
). The points
(x_{1},y_{1})
and
(x_{2},y_{2})
satisfy one of the following three possibilities:
h(x_{1},y_{1})<h(x_{2},y_{2})
,
h(x_{1},y_{1})>h(x_{2},y_{2})
, or
h(x_{1},y_{1})=h(x_{2},y_{2})
.
The case
h(x_{1},y_{1})=h(x_{2},y_{2})
follows from the results of the other two cases and Lemma
4.2
; see Subsection
7.5
.
The second case,
h(x_{1},y_{1})>h(x_{2},y_{2})
, can be reduced to the first by relabeling the points.
Thus, we focus on the first case.

7.1
Setup

The assumption

h(x_{1},y_{1})<h(x_{2},y_{2})

(7.2)

is equivalent to

(x_{2}-x_{1})+\mu(y_{2}-y_{1})>0\quad\text{where $\mu:=\frac{\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D}}{\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D}}$.}

(7.3)

We use the notation

h_{i}:=h(x_{i},y_{i})=\frac{1}{2}\left[x_{i}(\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D})+y_{i}(\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D})\right]

(7.4)

for
i=1,2
. Let
\mathrm{r}_{1},\mathrm{r}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
be fixed numbers as in Theorem
1.5
.

We again use
L
as the large parameter instead of
N
.
For every
L>0
, set

\begin{split}M_{L,i}=\lceil x_{i}aL\rceil,\qquad N_{L,i}=\lceil y_{i}bL\rceil,\qquad T_{L,i}=h_{i}L+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{i}L^{1/2}\qquad\text{for $i=1,2$,}\end{split}

(7.5)

and
M_{L,3}=\lceil aL\rceil
,
N_{L,3}=\lceil bL\rceil
,
T_{L,3}=\ell L
, with
\mathsf{\sigma}
in (
1.14
).
We also set
M_{L,0}=N_{L,0}=T_{L,0}=0
.
Note that
0<T_{L,1}<T_{L,2}<T_{L,3}
for all large enough
L
, and we always assume that
L
is large enough so that these inequalities hold.
Thus, Proposition
3.1
implies that

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{L}(M_{L,i},N_{L,i})>T_{L,i},\,i=1,2\,\big|\,\mathcal{L}(aL,bL)=\ell L\right)=\frac{\mathsf{Q}_{3}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})}{\mathsf{Q}_{1}(\lceil aL\rceil,\lceil bL\rceil,\ell L)}

where
\mathbf{M}_{L}=(M_{L,1},M_{L,2},M_{L,3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
,
\mathbf{N}_{L}=(N_{L,1},N_{L,2},N_{L,3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
, and
\mathbf{T}_{L}=(T_{L,1},T_{L,2},T_{L,3})\in{\mathbb{R}}_{+}^{3}
.
The goal is to prove that, with
\mathsf{c}_{+}
as in (
1.14
),

\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{\mathsf{Q}_{3}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})}{\mathsf{Q}_{1}(aL,bL,\ell L)}=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{i}-x_{i}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{i},i=1,2\right],

(7.6)

where
\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}
is a standard Brownian bridge.

From Proposition
3.1
,

\mathsf{Q}_{3}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})=\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{n}!)^{2}}\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}\qquad\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}:=\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{3}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}).

(7.7)

Noting that
m=3
, we have

\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}=\frac{(-1)^{|\mathbf{n}|}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\oint_{>1}\oint_{>1}\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}(z_{1},z_{2})\prod_{i=1}^{2}\frac{(z_{i}+1)^{n_{i}-n_{i+1}-1}}{z_{i}^{n_{i+1}+1}}\mathrm{d}z_{i},

(7.8)

where
\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}(z_{1},z_{2}):=\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\mathbf{z})
is given by

\begin{split}\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}(z_{1},z_{2})=&\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2|\mathbf{n}|}}\prod_{i=2}^{3}\prod_{k_{i}=1}^{n_{i}}\left[\int_{C_{i,\textnormal{left}}^{\textnormal{in}}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{k_{i}}^{i}+z_{i-1}\int_{C_{i,\textnormal{left}}^{\textnormal{out}}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{k_{i}}^{i}\right]\left[\int_{C_{i,\textnormal{right}}^{\textnormal{in}}}\mathrm{d}\eta_{k_{i}}^{i}+z_{i-1}\int_{C_{i,\textnormal{right}}^{\textnormal{out}}}\mathrm{d}\eta_{k_{i}}^{i}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\prod_{k_{1}=1}^{n_{1}}\left[\int_{C_{1,\textnormal{left}}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{k_{1}}^{1}\right]\left[\int_{C_{1,\textnormal{right}}}\mathrm{d}\eta_{k_{1}}^{1}\right]\,\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}).\end{split}

(7.9)

Here, recalling (
3.5
) and (
3.7
),

\begin{split}\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1}|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1})\left[\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{i},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{i+1}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{i},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{i+1})\right]\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3})\mathrm{S}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3})\end{split}

(7.10)

and, with the functions
f_{M,N,T}(z)=e^{N\log z-M\log(z+1)+Tz}
from (
3.6
),

{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):={\mathsf{F}}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\prod_{i=1}^{3}\prod_{k_{i}=1}^{n_{i}}\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(\xi_{k_{i}}^{i})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(\eta_{k_{i}}^{i})},\qquad{\mathsf{f}}_{L,i}(z):=\frac{f_{M_{L,i},N_{L,i},T_{L,i}}(z)}{f_{M_{L,i-1},N_{L,i-1},T_{L,i-1}}(z)}

(7.11)

with
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3})
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}=(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{2},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3})
, where

\boldsymbol{\xi}^{i}=(\xi^{i}_{1},\cdots,\xi^{i}_{n_{i}})
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{i}=(\eta^{i}_{1},\cdots,\eta^{i}_{n_{i}})
for
i=1,2,3
.
Note that for each
i=1,2,3
, the functions
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
and
\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
are symmetric in the variables
\xi^{i}_{1},\cdots,\xi^{i}_{n_{i}}
and also symmetric in
\eta^{i}_{1},\cdots,\eta^{i}_{n_{i}}
.

The rational function
\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
has simple poles at
\xi^{i}_{j}=\xi^{i+1}_{k}
and
\eta^{i}_{j}=\eta^{i+1}_{k}
for every
i,j,k
.
We will need to consider the residues at these various poles. The resulting expressions involve new functions

{\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(z):={\mathsf{f}}_{L,1}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z),\quad{\mathsf{f}}_{L,23}(z):={\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(z),\quad{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(z):={\mathsf{f}}_{L,1}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(z).

(7.12)

We observe that (cf. Subsection
6.1
)

{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}(z)=e^{\mathrm{G}_{*}(z)L+\mathrm{H}_{*}(z)L^{1/2}+\mathrm{E}_{L,*}(z)},\qquad*\in\{1,2,3,12,23,123\},

(7.13)

where

\begin{split}&\mathrm{G}_{1}(z)=-ax_{1}\log(z+1)+by_{1}\log z+h_{1}z,\\
&\mathrm{G}_{2}(z)=-a(x_{2}-x_{1})\log(z+1)+b(y_{2}-y_{1})\log z+(h_{2}-h_{1})z,\\
&\mathrm{G}_{3}(z)=-a(1-x_{2})\log(z+1)+b(1-y_{2})\log z+(\ell-h_{2})z\end{split}

(7.14)

and

\begin{split}&\mathrm{G}_{12}(z):=\mathrm{G}_{1}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z)=-ax_{2}\log(z+1)+by_{2}\log z+h_{2}z,\\
&\mathrm{G}_{23}(z):=\mathrm{G}_{2}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z)=-a(1-x_{1})\log(z+1)+b(1-y_{1})\log z+(\ell-h_{1})z,\\
&\mathrm{G}_{123}(z):=\mathrm{G}_{1}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z)=-a\log(z+1)+b\log z+\ell z.\end{split}

(7.15)

The functions
\mathrm{H}_{*}
are given by

\begin{split}&\mathrm{H}_{1}(z)=\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}z,\qquad\mathrm{H}_{2}(z)=\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})z,\qquad\mathrm{H}_{3}(z)=-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}z,\\
&\mathrm{H}_{12}(z):=\mathrm{H}_{1}(z)+\mathrm{H}_{2}(z)=\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}z,\qquad\mathrm{H}_{23}(z):=\mathrm{H}_{2}(z)+\mathrm{H}_{3}(z)=-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}z,\end{split}

(7.16)

and
\mathrm{H}_{123}(z):=\mathrm{H}_{1}(z)+\mathrm{H}_{2}(z)+\mathrm{H}_{3}(z)=0
.
Finally, the functions
\mathrm{E}_{L,*}
are

\begin{split}\mathrm{E}_{L,*}(z)=-\delta^{1}_{L,*}\log(z+1)+\delta^{2}_{L,*}\log z\end{split}

(7.17)

with real numbers satisfying

\delta^{1}_{L,*},\delta^{2}_{L,*}\in[-3,3]

(7.18)

so that
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}(z)
are meromorphic with possible poles only at
z=-1
and
z=0
.
All six functions
f_{L,*}
,
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
, are of the form (
5.2
). In Lemma
7.7
in Subsection
7.3
, we check the applicability of Lemma
5.3
to these functions.

7.2
Integrals

We will express the integrals appearing in
(
7.9
)
as sums of contributions from various residues.
To this end, we introduce notation for the types of integrals that will appear in these expressions.

Definition 7.1
.

Define the set

\mathcal{A}_{3}=\{1,2,3,12,23,123\}.

For
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
, define
\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
to be the set of lists
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\cdots\sigma_{k}
of elements
\sigma_{j}\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
such that, for each
i=1,2,3
, the total
number of times
i
appears in any of
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\cdots,\sigma_{k}
is equal to
n_{i}
. We denote

|{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|=k\qquad\text{if ${\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}\cdots\sigma_{k}$.}

Let
\mathcal{S}=\cup_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}}\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
.
The type of a list
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathcal{S}
is the vector

\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})=(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{6}

(7.19)

where
a_{*}
is the number of
\sigma_{i}
in
\boldsymbol{\sigma}=\sigma_{1}\cdots\sigma_{k}
such that
\sigma_{i}=*
for each
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
.

Typically, we write a list
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
as

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\alpha_{1}^{m_{1}}\alpha_{2}^{m_{2}}\alpha_{3}^{m_{3}}\cdots

(7.20)

where for each
i
,
\alpha_{i}
and
\alpha_{i+1}
are distinct elements of
\mathcal{A}_{3}
, and
\alpha^{m}
denotes the list consisting of
m

consecutive copies of
\alpha
.
If there is a possibility of confusion, we use parentheses for the numbers
12
,
23
, and
123
, writing them as
(12)
,
(23)
, or
(123)
, respectively. We also omit the superscript
1
when
m_{i}=1
.
For example,
3^{2}(23)^{1}1^{2}2^{1}=3^{2}(23)1^{2}2=33(23)112
is an element of
\mathcal{S}_{(2,2,3)}
of type
(0,0,1,2,1,2)
.
Similarly,
3^{2}(12)^{2}3=33(12)(12)3
is also an element of
\mathcal{S}_{(2,2,3)}
but of type
(0,2,0,0,0,3)
.
We have

\mathcal{S}_{(1,1,1)}=\{123,132,213,231,312,321,1(23),(23)1,(12)3,3(12),(123)\}.

Note that if
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
has
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})=\mathbf{a}=(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})
, then

a_{1}+a_{12}+a_{123}=n_{1},\quad a_{2}+a_{12}+a_{23}+a_{123}=n_{2},\quad a_{3}+a_{23}+a_{123}=n_{3}.

(7.21)

Definition 7.2
.

Let
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
. For
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
, define the functions

\begin{split}\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=&\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{123},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{12},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1}|\boldsymbol{\xi}^{123},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{12},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1})\,\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{2}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{23},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2})\,\\
&\times\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{12},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{12},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{2},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3})\,\mathrm{K}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{123},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{23},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{123},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3})\mathrm{S}(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{123},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{23},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3}|\boldsymbol{\eta}^{123},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3}),\end{split}

(7.22)

and

{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{*}}{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}(\xi_{i}^{*})}{\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\prod_{i=1}^{b_{*}}{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}(\eta_{i}^{*})}

(7.23)

where

(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})=\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}),\qquad(b_{123},b_{12},b_{23},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})=\type({\boldsymbol{\tau}}).

Here,
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{123},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{12},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{23},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3})

and
\boldsymbol{\eta}=(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{123},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{12},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{2},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3})
,
with
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{*}=(\xi^{*}_{1},\cdots,\xi^{*}_{a_{*}})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{a_{*}}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}=(\eta^{*}_{1},\cdots,\eta^{*}_{b_{*}})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{b_{*}}

for each
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
.

We note that
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
and
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
depend only on
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})
and
\type({\boldsymbol{\tau}})
, and not on the exact form of
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
.

The first
\mathrm{K}
and the last
\mathrm{K}
in (
7.22
) are determinants of Cauchy matrices of sizes
n_{1}
and
n_{3}
, respectively.
The second
\mathrm{K}
is the determinant of a Cauchy matrix of size
n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1}
, which is equal to
a_{1}+b_{23}+b_{2}
and also to
b_{1}+a_{23}+a_{2}
since
a_{1}-a_{2}-a_{23}=n_{1}-n_{2}=b_{1}-b_{2}-b_{23}
.
Similarly, the third
\mathrm{K}
is the determinant of a Cauchy matrix of size
n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3}
which is equal to
a_{12}+a_{2}+b_{3}
and also to
b_{12}+b_{2}+a_{3}
since

a_{3}-a_{2}-a_{12}=n_{3}-n_{2}=b_{3}-b_{2}-b_{12}
.

We note that for each
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
, the functions
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
and
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
are symmetric functions in the variables
\xi^{*}_{1},\cdots,\xi^{*}_{a_{*}}
and also symmetric in
\eta^{*}_{1},\cdots,\eta^{*}_{b_{*}}
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
with
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})=\mathbf{a}
.
For
\boldsymbol{\xi}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|\mathbf{a}|}
,
we define
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|\mathbf{a}|}

as follows.
We can always write
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}={\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{1}\cdots{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{r}
, where each sub-list

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}=123^{s^{123}_{i}}12^{s^{12}_{i}}23^{s^{23}_{i}}1^{s^{1}_{i}}2^{s^{2}_{i}}3^{s^{3}_{i}}
with
s_{i}^{*}\geq 0
for every
i
and for each superscript
*
.
Define

\boldsymbol{\xi}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\xi}_{r})

where, setting
k^{*}_{i}=s^{*}_{1}+\cdots+s^{*}_{i-1}
with
k^{*}_{1}=0
,

\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}=(\underbrace{\xi_{k^{123}_{i}+1}^{123},\cdots,\xi_{k^{123}_{i}+s^{123}_{i}}^{123}}_{s^{123}_{i}},\underbrace{\xi_{k^{12}_{i}+1}^{12},\cdots,\xi_{k^{12}_{i}+s^{12}_{i}}^{12}}_{s^{12}_{i}},\cdots,\underbrace{\xi_{k^{3}_{i}+1}^{3},\cdots,\xi_{k^{3}_{i}+s^{3}_{i}}^{3}}_{s^{3}_{i}})

for each
i
.
For example,

\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2(23)1}=(\xi^{2}_{1},\xi^{23}_{1},\xi^{1}_{1})
and

\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2(12)22}=(\xi^{2}_{1},\xi^{12}_{1},\xi^{2}_{2},\xi^{2}_{3})
.

Definition 7.3
.

For
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
and
L>0
, define the integral

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{|{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}|}}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}\,\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})

(7.24)

where the contour for
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}
is a product of
|{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|
small circles centered at
-1
, nested from inside to outside,
and the contour for
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
is a product of
|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}|
small circles centered at
0
, also nested from inside to outside. All circles are mutually disjoint.

For example, both

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=22(123)
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=2(12)32
are elements of
\mathcal{S}_{(1,3,1)}
, with
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})=(1,0,0,0,2,0)
and
\type({\boldsymbol{\tau}})=(0,1,0,0,2,1)
. We have

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{7}}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{2}_{1}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{2}_{2}\int_{\gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{123}_{1}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{2}_{1}\int_{\Gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{12}_{1}\int_{\Gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{3}_{1}\int_{\Gamma_{4}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{2}_{2}\,\,\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})

where
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}
are nested circles centered at
-1
of radii
0<r_{1}<r_{2}<r_{3}<1/2
,

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3},\Gamma_{4}
are nested circles centered at
0
of radii
0<R_{1}<R_{2}<R_{3}<R_{4}<1/2
; all circles are mutually disjoint.
Note that in this example, we can take
\gamma_{1}=\gamma_{2}
without changing the integral since the integrand is analytic at
\xi_{1}^{2}=\xi_{2}^{2}
. However, we cannot take
\Gamma_{3}
and
\Gamma_{4}
to be the same since
\eta^{3}_{1}=\eta^{2}_{2}
is a pole of
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
, arising from the third
\mathrm{K}
term in (
7.22
).

In terms of the notations introduced above, (
7.8
) can be written as follows.

Lemma 7.4
.

For
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
and
L>0
,

\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}=(-1)^{|\mathbf{n}|}\sum_{i=0\vee(2n_{2}-n_{1}+1)}^{2n_{2}}\sum_{j=0\vee(2n_{3}-n_{2}+1)}^{2n_{3}}\left[\oint_{>1}\frac{(z_{1}+1)^{n_{1}-n_{2}-1}}{z_{1}^{n_{2}-i+1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{1}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\right]\left[\oint_{>1}\frac{(z_{2}+1)^{n_{2}-n_{3}-1}}{z_{2}^{n_{3}-j+1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z_{2}}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\right]\alpha_{ij}

where
\alpha_{ij}
is a sum of
\binom{2n_{2}}{i}\binom{2n_{3}}{j}
terms, each of the form
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
with
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
specified by

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}1^{n_{1}}2^{n_{22}}3^{n_{32}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{n_{31}^{\prime}}2^{n^{\prime}_{21}}1^{n_{1}}2^{n_{22}^{\prime}}3^{n_{32}^{\prime}}

(7.25)

for
n_{21},n_{22},n_{31},n_{32},n_{21}^{\prime},n_{22}^{\prime},n_{31}^{\prime},n_{32}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
subject to the constraints

\begin{split}n_{21}+n_{22}=n_{21}^{\prime}+n_{22}^{\prime}=n_{2},\quad n_{31}+n_{32}=n_{31}^{\prime}+n_{32}^{\prime}=n_{3},\quad n_{22}+n^{\prime}_{22}=i,\quad n_{32}+n_{32}^{\prime}=j.\end{split}

Proof.

Multiplying out the formula (
7.9
), and using the invariance of
\Pi_{\mathbf{n}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
and
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
under suitable permutations of the variables,
we find that

\mathsf{D}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}(z_{1},z_{2})=\sum_{i=0}^{2n_{2}}\sum_{j=0}^{2n_{3}}\alpha_{ij}z_{1}^{i}z_{2}^{j}

(7.26)

where
\alpha_{ij}
is a sum of
\binom{2n_{2}}{i}\binom{2n_{3}}{j}
terms of the form
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
with
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
as in (
7.25
).
Inserting this formula into (
7.8
), we obtain the result since

\oint\frac{(z+1)^{n-n^{\prime}-1}}{z^{n^{\prime}-i+1}}\mathrm{d}z=0
whenever
i\leq 2n^{\prime}-n
.
∎

In Subsection
7.7
, the integrals
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
with
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
as in (
7.25
) will be further rewritten in terms of
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
with other choices of
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
, which are more amenable to the application of the method of steepest descent.

7.3
Critical point analysis

We now consider the critical points of the functions
\mathrm{G}_{L,*}
in (
7.14
) and (
7.15
), and these will be used in the asymptotic evaluation of the integrals
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
.
All these functions are special cases of the function given in (
7.27
) below.

As before, let
a,b>0
and
\ell>\bar{\mathcal{L}}(a,b)
be fixed. We set
D=\ell^{2}-2(a+b)\ell+(a-b)^{2}
,
\mathrm{m}=\frac{\ell-a-b+\sqrt{D}}{\ell-a-b-\sqrt{D}}
, and
\mu=\frac{\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D}}{\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D}}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

For every
X,Y\in{\mathbb{R}}\setminus\{0\}
, the critical points of the function

\mathrm{G}_{X,Y}(z)=-aX\log(1+z)+bY\log z+\frac{1}{2}\left[X(\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D})+Y(\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D})\right]z

(7.27)

are

\mathsf{w}_{c}=-\frac{\frac{Y}{X}}{\frac{1}{\mu}+\frac{Y}{X}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathsf{z}_{c}=-\frac{\ell-a+b-\sqrt{D}}{2\ell}.

(7.28)

Furthermore, they satisfy the following properties:

1.

If
\frac{Y}{X}<-\frac{1}{\mu}
, then
\mathsf{w}_{c}<-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}<0
.

2.

If
-\frac{1}{\mu}<\frac{Y}{X}<0
, then
-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}<0<\mathsf{w}_{c}
.

3.

If
0<\frac{Y}{X}<\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
, then
-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}<\mathsf{w}_{c}<0
.

4.

If
\frac{Y}{X}>\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
, then
-1<\mathsf{w}_{c}<\mathsf{z}_{c}<0
.

Furthermore,

\frac{\mathrm{G}_{X,Y}^{\prime\prime}(\mathsf{z}_{c})}{\mathrm{G}_{1,1}^{\prime\prime}(\mathsf{z}_{c})}=\frac{\mathrm{m}Y-X}{\mathrm{m}-1}.

(7.29)

Proof.

This lemma in principle follows from Lemma
5.2
. However, the explicit computation of the critical points can be tedious using the formula (
5.4
). Instead we proceed as follows. We have

\mathrm{G}_{X,Y}^{\prime}(z)=X\left[-\frac{a}{1+z}+\frac{\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D}}{2}\right]+Y\left[\frac{b}{z}+\frac{\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D}}{2}\right].

Noting that
\frac{1}{1+\mathsf{z}_{c}}=\frac{\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D}}{2a}
and
\frac{1}{\mathsf{z}_{c}}=-\frac{\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D}}{2b}
, we see that
\mathrm{G}_{X,Y}^{\prime}(\mathsf{z}_{c})=0
.
Also, since

\mathrm{G}_{X,Y}^{\prime}(z)=\frac{hz^{2}+(bY-aX+h)z+bY}{z(1+z)}\qquad\text{where $h:=\frac{1}{2}[X(\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D})+Y(\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D})]$,}

we find that
\mathrm{G}
has another critical point given by

\mathsf{w}_{c}=\frac{bY}{h\mathsf{z}_{c}}=-\frac{\frac{Y}{X}}{\frac{1}{\mu}+\frac{Y}{X}}.

The conditions
\ell>\bar{\mathcal{L}}(a,b)=(\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b})^{2}
and
a,b>0
imply that
\ell\pm(a-b)\pm\sqrt{D}>0
for all four choices of signs.
These inequalities show that
-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}<0
.
Properties (a) and (b) follow directly from the formula of
\mathsf{w}_{c}
.
Properties (c) and (d) are obtained by noting that
\mathsf{w}_{c}=\mathsf{z}_{c}
if and only if
\frac{Y}{X}=\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
.
Finally, (
7.29
) can be derived by direct computation.
∎

Recalling (
7.4
), the functions
\mathrm{G}_{1},\mathrm{G}_{2},\mathrm{G}_{3},\mathrm{G}_{12},\mathrm{G}_{23}
, and
\mathrm{G}_{123}
are all equal to the function
\mathrm{G}_{X,Y}(z)
in (
7.27
) with the parameters

(X,Y)=(x_{1},y_{1}),\quad(x_{2}-x_{1},y_{2}-y_{1}),\quad(1-x_{2},1-y_{2}),\quad(x_{2},y_{2}),\quad(1-x_{1},1-y_{1}),\quad(1,1),

(7.30)

respectively.
The ordering of their critical points depends on the relative positions of
(x_{1},y_{1})
and
(x_{2},y_{2})
.
Set

\mathsf{q}=(x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}).

(7.31)

From the assumptions (
7.1
) and (
7.3
),
\mathsf{q}
belongs to the region

\mathsf{R}=\left\{(x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2})\in(0,1)^{4}:\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1\text{ and }(x_{2}-x_{1})+\mu(y_{2}-y_{1})>0\right\}.

(7.32)

Since
(x_{1},y_{1})\in(0,1)^{2}
satisfies
\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}<1
, we find that

-\frac{1}{\mu}<0<\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}<1<\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}.

(7.33)

The six numbers above divide the real line into seven intervals.
We define the following seven disjoint sub-regions of
\mathsf{R}
according to which interval the value
\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}
belongs to:

\begin{split}&\mathsf{R}_{1}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<-\frac{1}{\mu}\right\},\quad\quad\quad\quad\mathsf{R}_{2}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}>\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}\right\},\\
&\mathsf{R}_{3}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:1<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}\right\},\quad\mathsf{R}_{4}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<1\right\},\\
&\mathsf{R}_{5}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}\right\},\quad\quad\mathsf{R}_{6}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:0<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}\right\},\\
&\mathsf{R}_{7}:=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:-\frac{1}{\mu}<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<0\right\}.\end{split}

(7.34)

The region
\mathsf{R}
is the union of these seven regions and a finite collection of hypersurfaces.
Since
(x_{2}-x_{1})+\mu(y_{2}-y_{1})>0
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}
, the regions
\mathsf{R}_{1}
and
\mathsf{R}_{7}
simplify to

\mathsf{R}_{1}=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:x_{2}<x_{1}\,\,y_{2}>y_{1}\right\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathsf{R}_{7}=\left\{\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}:x_{2}>x_{1}\,\,y_{2}<y_{1}\right\}.

(7.35)

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
, we have
x_{2}-x_{1}>0
and
y_{2}-y_{1}>0
.

We now state the following result regarding the orderings of the critical points.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
\mathsf{z}_{c}=-\frac{\ell-a+b-\sqrt{D}}{2\ell}
, as in Lemma
7.5
.
Let
z_{*}^{-}\leq z_{*}^{+}
denote the critical points of
\mathrm{G}_{*}
for each
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
. Then, for every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

z^{+}_{1}=z^{+}_{3}=z^{+}_{12}=z^{+}_{23}=z_{123}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}.

(7.36)

Furthermore, the following results hold:

1.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, then
z^{-}_{2}<-1<z^{-}_{23}<z^{-}_{3}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{1}<\mathsf{z}_{c}=z^{+}_{2}<0
.

2.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}
, then
-1<z^{-}_{2}<z^{-}_{23}<z^{-}_{3}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{1}<\mathsf{z}_{c}=z^{+}_{2}<0
.

3.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}
, then
-1<z^{-}_{3}<z^{-}_{23}<z^{-}_{2}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{1}<\mathsf{z}_{c}=z^{+}_{2}<0
.

4.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{4}
, then
-1<z^{-}_{3}<z^{-}_{23}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{2}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{1}<\mathsf{z}_{c}=z^{+}_{2}<0
.

5.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
, then
-1<z^{-}_{3}<z^{-}_{23}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{1}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{2}<\mathsf{z}_{c}=z^{+}_{2}<0
.

6.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}
, then
-1<z^{-}_{3}<z^{-}_{23}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{1}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{2}=\mathsf{z}_{c}<z^{+}_{2}<0
.

7.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then
-1<z^{-}_{3}<z^{-}_{23}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{1}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{2}=\mathsf{z}_{c}<0<z^{+}_{2}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
7.5
, one of the critical points is
\mathsf{z}_{c}
for every
\mathrm{G}_{*}
; this critical point does not depend on
\mathsf{q}
.
Since
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}
, we have

\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{i}}{x_{i}}<1<\frac{1-y_{i}}{1-x_{i}}

(7.37)

for both
i=1,2
.
Thus, the parameters
(X,Y)
in (
7.30
) satisfy
Y,X>0
and
\frac{Y}{X}>\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
for every
*\neq 2
.
Hence, Lemma
7.5
(d) implies that
\mathsf{z}_{c}
is the larger critical point of
\mathrm{G}_{*}
for
*\neq 2
, implying (
7.36
), and the smaller critical points are:

\begin{split}z^{-}_{1}=-\frac{\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}},\quad z^{-}_{3}=-\frac{\frac{1-y_{2}}{1-x_{2}}}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+\frac{1-y_{2}}{1-x_{2}}},\quad z^{-}_{12}=-\frac{\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}},\quad z^{-}_{23}=-\frac{\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}},\quad z^{-}_{123}=-\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+1}.\end{split}

Note that the function
r\mapsto-\frac{r}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+r}
is decreasing in
r
.
Hence, using (
7.37
) we find that for every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

\max\{z_{23}^{-},z_{3}^{-}\}<z^{-}_{123}<\min\{z_{1}^{-},z_{12}^{-}\}.

From (
7.33
) and the definitions of the sub-regions, we see that

\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}

and

\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}>\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
.

Using (
7.35
), these inequalities imply that
(y_{2}-y_{1})x_{1}>(x_{2}-x_{1})y_{1}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
,
and
(y_{2}-y_{1})x_{1}<(x_{2}-x_{1})y_{1}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
. Hence,
\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}>\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}
in the former case, and
\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}
in the latter case, implying that
z_{12}^{-}<z_{1}^{-}
in the former case and
z_{12}^{-}>z_{1}^{-}
in the latter case.
Similarly, also from the definitions of the sub-regions, we see that

\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup(\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7})
,

and

\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}>\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}
.

These inequalities imply that
\frac{1-y_{2}}{1-x_{2}}<\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
, and
\frac{1-y_{2}}{1-x_{2}}>\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
. Thus,
z_{3}^{-}>z_{23}^{-}
in the former case and
z_{3}^{-}<z_{23}^{-}
in the latter case.
Thus, we have obtained all inequalities for the critical points that do not involve
z_{2}^{\pm}
.

We now consider
z_{2}^{\pm}
.
Let
w_{c}=-\frac{\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}}{\frac{1}{\mu_{c}}+\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}}
.
From Lemma
7.5
, we have

\begin{split}&z_{2}^{-}=w_{c},\quad z_{2}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}$;}\qquad z_{2}^{-}=\mathsf{z}_{c},\quad z_{2}^{+}=w_{c}\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$.}\\
\end{split}

Furthermore,
w_{c}<-1
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
,
w_{c}>0
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
,
w_{c}\in(\mathsf{z}_{c},0)
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}
, and
w_{c}\in(-1,\mathsf{z}_{c})
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
.
Since
\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}>\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}
for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{2}
and
1<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}}
for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{3}
,
we find that
w_{c}<z_{23}^{-}
in the former case and
w_{2}\in(z_{23}^{-},z_{123}^{-})
in the latter case.
Finally, observe that if
x_{2}-x_{1}>0
, then

\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}}<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}
if and only if
y_{1}x_{2}<y_{2}x_{1}
, which is equivalent to
\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}
.
Thus,
\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<1
for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{4}
, and
\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}<\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}
for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
, so that
w_{c}\in(z_{123}^{-},z_{12}^{-})
in the former case and
w_{c}>z_{12}^{-}
in the latter.
This completes the proof.
∎

The functions
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}
are of the form (
5.2
).
We conclude this subsection by verifying that Lemma
5.3
is applicable to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}
.

Lemma 7.7
.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then Lemma
5.3

1
and
2
apply to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}(z)
for every
*\in\{1,3,12,23,123\}
.
Moreover, if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
, then Lemma
5.3

2
applies to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z)
; if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then Lemma
5.3

1
applies to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z)
.

Proof.

The parameters
S_{*}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3})
in (
5.1
) for
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,*}
are given by

\begin{split}&S_{1}=(ax_{1},by_{1},h_{1}),\quad S_{2}=(a(x_{2}-x_{1}),b(y_{2}-y_{1}),h_{2}-h_{1}),\quad S_{3}=(a(1-x_{2}),b(1-y_{2}),\ell-h_{2}),\\
&S_{12}=(ax_{2},by_{2},h_{2}),\quad S_{23}=(a(1-x_{1}),b(1-y_{1}),\ell-h_{1}),\quad S_{123}=(a,b,\ell),\end{split}

where the
h_{i}
are given in (
7.4
). In all cases,
\alpha_{3}=h(\alpha_{1}/a,\alpha_{2}/b)
, where (see (
1.10
))

h(x,y)=\frac{1}{2}\left[(\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D})x+(\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D})y\right].

Consider
*\neq 2
.
Since
x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\in(0,1)
, we have
0<h_{1},h_{2}<\ell
, and thus
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}>0
in all relevant cases.
For
x,y>0
, the arithmetic-geometric mean inequality implies that

\hat{h}(x,y):=(\ell-a-b-\sqrt{D})x+(\ell-a-b+\sqrt{D})y>4\sqrt{abxy}\qquad\text{if}\quad\frac{y}{x}\neq\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}.

Thus,
h(x,y)=\frac{1}{2}\hat{h}(x,y)+ax+by>(\sqrt{ax}+\sqrt{by})^{2}
if
\frac{y}{x}\neq\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
. Therefore,

\alpha_{3}=h(\alpha_{1}/a,\alpha_{2}/b)>(\sqrt{\alpha_{1}}+\sqrt{\alpha_{2}})^{2}\quad\text{if}\quad\frac{a\alpha_{2}}{b\alpha_{1}}\neq\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}.

By definition (cf. (
7.33
)),
\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}},\frac{1-y_{1}}{1-x_{1}},\frac{1-y_{2}}{1-x_{2}}>\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
, and thus
\frac{a\alpha_{2}}{b\alpha_{1}}\neq\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
.
Hence,
\alpha_{3}>(\sqrt{\alpha_{1}}+\sqrt{\alpha_{2}})^{2}
for all
*\neq 2
.
Therefore, Lemma
5.3

1
and
2
hold.

Consider
*=2
.
Then, by assumption (
7.2
),
\alpha_{3}=h_{2}-h_{1}>0
.
From (
7.35
),
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}>0
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
.
From (
7.33
) and (
7.34
), we also see that
\frac{y_{2}-y_{1}}{x_{2}-x_{1}}\neq\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}
for all
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Hence, the argument of the previous paragraph applies, and we find that Lemma
5.3

1
and
2
apply to
f_{L,2}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
.
Additionally, from (
7.35
), we have
\alpha_{1}<0,\alpha_{2}>0
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, and
\alpha_{1}>0,\alpha_{2}<0
for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Therefore, if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, then Lemma
5.3

2
holds, and if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then Lemma
5.3

1
holds.
∎

7.4
Leading contributions

In this section, we evaluate several integrals, which we will later show provide the leading contributions to the limit.

Consider
\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(1,1,1)}
in (
7.7
). From Lemma
3.3
with
m=3
, we have

\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}=-\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{6}}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{1}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{2}\int_{\gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{3}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{1}\int_{\Gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{2}\int_{\Gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}\eta^{3}\,\,\Pi_{(1,1,1)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}^{(1,1,1)}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})

where
\gamma_{1}
,
\gamma_{2}
,
\gamma_{3}
are small circles around the point
z=-1
, nested from inside to outside, and
\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma_{2}
,
\Gamma_{3}
are small circles around the point
z=0
, also nested from inside to outside; all circles are disjoint. Using the notations of Subsection
7.2
,

\begin{split}\Pi_{\mathbf{1}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})&=\mathrm{K}_{1}(\eta^{1}|\xi^{1})\mathrm{K}_{2}(\xi^{1},\eta^{2}|\eta^{1},\xi^{2})\mathrm{K}_{2}(\xi^{2},\eta^{3}|\eta^{2},\xi^{3})\mathrm{K}_{1}(\xi^{3}|\eta^{3})\mathrm{S}_{1}(\xi^{3}|\eta^{3})=\Pi^{123}_{123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\end{split}

and
{\mathsf{F}}^{(\mathbf{1})}_{\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})={\mathsf{F}}^{123|123}_{L}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
. Thus,

\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}=-\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}.

Note that
\Pi^{123}_{123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
is a rational function, and
{\mathsf{F}}^{123|123}_{L}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
is analytic except possibly at
-1
and
0
.
We deform the contours and repeatedly apply Cauchy’s residue theorem. For example, for fixed
\eta^{1},\eta^{2},\eta^{3}
, if we swap the contours for
\xi^{2}
and
\xi^{3}
, then
due to the pole at
\xi^{2}=\xi^{3}
,

\begin{split}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{1}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{2}\int_{\gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{3}\,\,\Pi^{123}_{123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}^{123|123}_{L}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\int_{\gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{1}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{3}\int_{\gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{2}\,\,\Pi^{123}_{123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}^{123|123}_{L}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\\
+2\pi\mathrm{i}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{1}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi^{23}\,\,\Pi^{1(23)}_{123}(\xi^{1},\xi^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}^{123|123}_{L}(\xi^{1},\xi^{23},\xi^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}),\end{split}

since, recalling the Cauchy determinant formula (
3.3
),

\begin{split}&\lim_{\xi^{2}\to\xi^{3}}(\xi^{2}-\xi^{3})\Pi^{123}_{123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\bigg|_{\xi^{3}=\xi^{23}}\\
&=\mathrm{K}(\eta^{1}|\xi^{1})\mathrm{K}(\xi^{1},\eta^{2}|\eta^{1},\xi^{23})\left[\lim_{\xi^{2}\to\xi^{3}}(\xi^{2}-\xi^{3})\mathrm{K}(\xi^{2},\eta^{3}|\eta^{2},\xi^{3})\right]_{\xi^{3}=\xi^{23}}\mathrm{K}(\xi^{23}|\eta^{3})\mathrm{S}(\xi^{23}|\eta^{3})\\
&=-\mathrm{K}(\eta^{1}|\xi^{1})\mathrm{K}(\xi^{1},\eta^{2}|\eta^{1},\xi^{23})\mathrm{K}(\eta^{3}|\eta^{2})\mathrm{K}(\xi^{23}|\eta^{3})\mathrm{S}(\xi^{23}|\eta^{3})=-\Pi^{1(23)}_{123}(\xi^{1},\xi^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}).\end{split}

Thus, we see that
\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}=\mathrm{I}^{132}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{1(23)}_{123}
.
By repeating similar procedures, we can represent the original integral in the following forms.

Lemma 7.8
.

We have
\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}=-\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}
, and the following expressions for
\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}
hold:

1.

\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}=\mathrm{I}^{231}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{3(12)}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
.

2.

\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}=\mathrm{I}^{321}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{(23)1}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{3(12)}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
.

3.

\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}=\mathrm{I}^{312}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{(23)1}_{123}+\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
.

4.

\mathrm{I}^{123}_{123}=\mathrm{I}^{312}_{213}+\mathrm{I}^{(23)1}_{213}+\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{213}-\mathrm{I}^{312}_{(12)3}-\mathrm{I}^{(23)1}_{(12)3}+\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}
.

From Lemma
7.6
, we see that the integrals on the right-hand side are suitable for applying the method of steepest descent in the following cases: (a) for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
; (b) for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
; (c) for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
; and (d) for
w\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, we evaluate
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
asymptotically in Proposition
7.10
below, and show in Proposition
7.11
that other integrals are sub-dominant.
Furthermore, in Corollary
7.15
, we show that
\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}
,
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1)
, give sub-dominating contributions.
The integral
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
is responsible for the limit in Theorem
1.5
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
.

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, we will show that

\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}
is the largest among the integrals appearing in Lemma
7.8
(d).
It turns out that another term from
\mathsf{Q}^{(1,2,1)}_{L}
also contributes to the limit in Theorem
1.5
.
By Lemma
3.4
, we have

\mathsf{Q}^{(1,2,1)}_{L}=\mathrm{I}^{3122}_{1223}+\mathrm{I}^{1223}_{3122}.

(7.38)

We write these as follows, similarly to the previous lemma. The proofs of this lemma and the previous one are tedious; therefore, we present a unified treatment of the deformations of the integrals in Subsection
7.7
.

Lemma 7.9
.

We have

\begin{split}\mathsf{Q}^{(1,2,1)}_{L}=&2\mathrm{I}^{3122}_{2231}+2\mathrm{I}^{3122}_{2(12)3}-2\mathrm{I}^{(23)12}_{2231}-2\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{2231}-2\mathrm{I}^{3122}_{23(12)}+4\mathrm{I}^{(23)12}_{23(12)}-4\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{23(12)}\\
&-2\mathrm{I}^{3122}_{2(23)1}-4\mathrm{I}^{(23)12}_{2(23)1}-4\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{2(23)1}-2\mathrm{I}^{3122}_{(23)(12)}-4\mathrm{I}^{(23)12}_{(23)(12)}+4\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}.\end{split}

When
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, the integral
\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}
has the same leading-order behavior as
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}
.
We will see that two integrals
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
and
\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}
are responsible for the limit in Theorem
1.5
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

In the remainder of this subsection, we evaluate the integral
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
asymptotically when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, and the integrals
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}
and
\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

Proposition 7.10
.

For
L>0
, define the constant (which is the same as (
6.7
))

\mathsf{Z}_{L}=\left(\frac{\ell+a-b+\sqrt{D}}{\ell+a-b-\sqrt{D}}\right)^{\lceil aL\rceil}\left(\frac{\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D}}{\ell-a+b-\sqrt{D}}\right)^{\lceil bL\rceil}e^{-\sqrt{D}L}.

(7.39)

As
L\to\infty
, the following hold:

\begin{split}&\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}=-\frac{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}{2\pi LD}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{i}-x_{i}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{i},i=1,2\right](1+o(1))\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}$,}\\
&\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}=-\frac{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}{2\pi LD}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{2}-x_{2}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{2}\right](1+o(1))\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$,}\\
&\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}=-\frac{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}{2\pi LD}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{1}-x_{1}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)\leq\mathrm{r}_{1},\,\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{2}-x_{2}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{2}\right](1+o(1))\quad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$.}\end{split}

Proof.

The analysis of three integrals is similar. In all cases, the critical points
z_{123}^{\pm}
of the function

\mathrm{G}_{123}(z)=-a\log(z+1)+b\log z+\ell z
play a distinguished role. We use the notations (see Lemma
7.5
)

\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}:=z_{123}^{-}=-\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\mu}+1}=-\frac{\ell-a+b+\sqrt{D}}{2\ell},\qquad\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}:=z_{123}^{+}=-\frac{\ell-a+b-\sqrt{D}}{2\ell}.

They satisfy
-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}<\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}<0
.
It is straightforward to check (cf. (
5.5
)) that

\mp\mathrm{G}_{123}^{\prime\prime}(z_{c}^{\pm})=\frac{\sqrt{D}}{2ab}\left[(a+b)\ell-(a-b)^{2}\pm(a-b)\sqrt{D}\right]=2\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}\mathsf{c}^{2}_{\pm}.

(7.40)

The integrals we consider are

\begin{split}&\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{4}}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\text{d}\xi^{123}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\text{d}\eta^{1}\int_{\Gamma_{2}}\text{d}\eta^{2}\int_{\Gamma_{3}}\text{d}\eta^{3}\,\,\Pi^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}),\\
&\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\text{d}\xi^{123}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\text{d}\eta^{12}\int_{\Gamma_{2}}\text{d}\eta^{3}\,\,\Pi^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})F_{L}^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}),\\
&\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{4}}\int_{\gamma_{1}}\text{d}\xi^{123}\int_{\gamma_{2}}\text{d}\xi^{2}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\text{d}\eta^{23}\int_{\Gamma_{2}}\text{d}\eta^{12}\,\,\Pi^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})F_{L}^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\end{split}

where
\boldsymbol{\xi}=\xi^{123}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}=(\eta^{1},\eta^{2},\eta^{3})
for the first integral;

\boldsymbol{\xi}=\xi^{123}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}=(\eta^{12},\eta^{3})
for the second integral; and

\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\xi^{123},\xi^{2})
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}=(\eta^{12},\eta^{23})
for the third integral.
The contours
\gamma_{i}
are small circles around
-1
, and
\Gamma_{i}
are small circles around
0
; all contours are chosen to be non-intersecting.
The circle
\gamma_{1}
is nested inside
\gamma_{2}
, and the circles
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3}
are nested from inside to outside.
The functions are

\begin{split}&\Pi^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):=\Pi^{(123)}_{123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{1}{(\eta^{1}-\xi^{123})(\eta^{2}-\eta^{1})(\eta^{3}-\eta^{2})},\\
&\Pi^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):=\Pi^{(123)}_{(12)3}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{1}{(\eta^{12}-\xi^{123})(\eta^{3}-\eta^{12})},\\
&\Pi^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):=\Pi^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{1}{(\eta^{12}-\xi^{123})(\eta^{23}-\xi^{2})(\xi^{2}-\eta^{12})},\end{split}

(7.41)

and

\begin{split}&{\mathsf{F}}^{(a)}_{L}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):={\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(123)|123}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\xi^{123})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,1}(\eta^{1}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(\eta^{2}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(\eta^{3})},\\
&F_{L}^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):=F_{L}^{(123)|(12)3}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\xi^{123})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(\eta^{12}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(\eta^{3})},\\
&F_{L}^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}):=F_{L}^{(123)2|(23)(12)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\xi^{123}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(\xi^{2})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(\eta^{12}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,23}(\eta^{23})}.\end{split}

Lemma
7.6
implies that the critical points satisfy

\begin{split}&-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}<z_{1}^{+}=z_{2}^{+}=z_{3}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}<0\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}$,}\\
&-1<\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}<z_{2}^{-}=z_{12}^{+}=z_{3}^{+}=z^{+}_{23}=\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}<0\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$.}\end{split}

Note that, since
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,1}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(z)={\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(z){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(z)={\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(z)\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,23}(z)}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(z)}={\mathsf{f}}_{123}(z)
, we have, in terms of (
7.39
),

\begin{split}\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,1}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})}=\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,3}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})}=\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}){\mathsf{f}}_{L,23}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})}=\frac{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-})}{{\mathsf{f}}_{L,123}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})}=\mathsf{Z}_{L}.\end{split}

(7.42)

We take the contours to be the circles given by

\begin{split}&\gamma_{1}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z+1|=1+\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}\},\\
&\gamma_{2}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z+1|=1+\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}\},\\
&\Gamma_{i}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z|=|\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}|-(4-i)L^{-1/2}\},\qquad i=1,2,3.\\
\end{split}

These contours satisfy the necessary nesting structure.
We now evaluate the integrals.
From the formula of the functions,

\Pi^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=O(L),\qquad\Pi^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=O(L^{1/2}),\qquad\Pi^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=O(L)

uniformly for
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contours.
Fix
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
and denote the disks

D_{-}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:|z-\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}|\leq L^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}}\},\qquad D_{+}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}:|z-\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}|\leq L^{-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{3}}\}.

Let
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}
be the part of the circle
\gamma
contained in the disk
D_{-}
.
Let
\gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
,
\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}
,
\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
,
\Gamma_{3}^{\epsilon}
denote the parts of the corresponding circles contained in the disk
D_{+}
.
Note that
z_{2}^{-}=\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}
, and
\gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
is a sub-arc of the circle
\gamma_{2}
near this critical point, when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Lemma
7.7
implies that Lemma
5.3

1
applies to
f_{L,123}
, and Lemma
5.3

2
applies to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,1},{\mathsf{f}}_{L,3},{\mathsf{f}}_{L,12}
, and
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,23}
.
Furthermore, Lemma
5.3

2
applies to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, while Lemma
5.3

1
applies to
{\mathsf{f}}_{L,2}
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Thus, we find that

\frac{{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=O(e^{-cL^{2\epsilon/3}}),\qquad\frac{{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=O(e^{-cL^{2\epsilon/3}}),\qquad\frac{{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=O(e^{-cL^{2\epsilon/3}})

for
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contours outside the parts
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{3}^{\epsilon}
, or
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
, or
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
, respectively.
On the other hand, for
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the parts
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{3}^{\epsilon}
, or
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
, or
\gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{1}^{\epsilon}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\epsilon}
(respectively),
we change variables as follows:

\eta^{*}=\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}+\frac{v_{*}}{L^{1/2}}\quad\text{for $*=1,2,3,12,23$},\qquad\xi^{123}=\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}+\frac{u}{L^{1/2}},\qquad\xi^{2}=\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}+\frac{v_{0}}{L^{1/2}}.

Noting
\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}-\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-}=\sqrt{D}/\ell
, we find from (
7.41
) that

\begin{split}\Pi^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{\ell L/\sqrt{D}(1+o(1))}{(v_{2}-v_{1})(v_{3}-v_{2})},\quad\Pi^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{\ell L^{1/2}/\sqrt{D}(1+o(1))}{v_{3}-v_{12}},\quad\Pi^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=\frac{\ell L/\sqrt{D}(1+o(1))}{(v_{23}-v_{0})(v_{0}-v_{12})}\end{split}

for variables
|u|,|v_{0}|,|v_{1}|,|v_{2}|,|v_{3}|,|v_{12}|,|v_{23}|\leq L^{\epsilon/3}
on appropriate contours.
Using Lemma
5.1
and recalling (
7.16
), we also find that

\begin{split}&\frac{{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{(a)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}u^{2}}}{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{1}v^{2}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}v_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{2}v^{2}_{2}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})v_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{3}v^{2}_{3}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{3}}}(1+o(1)),\\
&\frac{F_{L}^{(b)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}u^{2}}}{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{3}v^{2}_{3}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{3}}}(1+o(1)),\\
&\frac{F_{L}^{(c)}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})}{\mathsf{Z}_{L}}=\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}u^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}_{2}v^{2}_{0}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})v_{0}}}{e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{23}v^{2}_{23}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}v_{23}}}(1+o(1))\end{split}

for the same variables, where we set

\mathrm{A}_{*}=-\mathrm{G}^{\prime\prime}_{*}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})>0\quad\text{for}\quad*=1,3,12,23,\quad\mathrm{B}=\mathrm{G}^{\prime\prime}_{123}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{-})=2\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}\mathsf{c}^{2}_{-}>0,\quad\mathrm{A}_{2}=-\mathrm{G}^{\prime\prime}_{2}(\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+})=-\mathrm{B}_{2}.

Note that
\mathrm{A}_{2}>0
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, since
\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}=z_{2}^{+}
in this case, and
\mathrm{B}_{2}>0
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, since
\mathsf{z}_{c}^{+}=z_{2}^{-}
in this case.
Hence, noting that
\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}
is equal to
L^{-2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v_{1}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\mathrm{d}v_{3}
,
L^{-3/2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v_{12}\mathrm{d}v_{3}
, or
L^{-2}\mathrm{d}u\mathrm{d}v_{0}\mathrm{d}v_{12}\mathrm{d}v_{23}
, respectively,
we conclude that

\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{L\sqrt{D}}{\ell\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}=P_{0}P_{a},\qquad\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{L\sqrt{D}}{\ell\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}=P_{0}P_{b},\qquad\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{L\sqrt{D}}{\ell\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}=P_{0}P_{c},\qquad

where

P_{0}=\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\int_{\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}u^{2}}\mathrm{d}u=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\mathrm{B}}}=\frac{1}{\mathsf{\sigma}\mathsf{c}_{-}\sqrt{4\pi}},

and

\begin{split}&P_{a}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\int_{3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{1}\int_{2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{2}\int_{1+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{3}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{1}v^{2}_{1}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}v_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{2}v^{2}_{2}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})v_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{3}v^{2}_{3}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{3}}}{(v_{2}-v_{1})(v_{3}-v_{2})},\\
&P_{b}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\int_{3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\mathrm{d}v_{12}\int_{2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\,\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{3}v^{2}_{3}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{3}}}{v_{3}-v_{12}},\\
&P_{c}=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{0}\int_{3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{23}\int_{2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{12}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}_{2}v^{2}_{0}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{23}v^{2}_{23}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}v_{23}}}{(v_{23}-v_{0})(v_{0}-v_{12})}.\end{split}

Here,
\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}
is oriented upward, while
1+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}
,
2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}
, and
3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}
are oriented downwards.

We now evaluate
P_{a}
,
P_{b}
, and
P_{c}
using Lemma
4.1
.
Noting that
\mathrm{G}_{1}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z)=\mathrm{G}_{12}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z)=\mathrm{G}_{12}(z)+\mathrm{G}_{23}(z)-\mathrm{G}_{2}(z)=\mathrm{G}_{123}(z)
, set

\mathrm{A}:=\mathrm{A}_{1}+\mathrm{A}_{2}+\mathrm{A}_{3}=\mathrm{A}_{12}+\mathrm{A}_{3}=\mathrm{A}_{12}+\mathrm{A}_{23}+\mathrm{B}_{2}=-\mathrm{G}^{\prime\prime}_{123}(z^{+}_{c})=2\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}\mathsf{c}^{2}_{+}.

Changing variables
v_{*}\mapsto-v_{*}
in
P_{a}
and
P_{b}
, we find from Lemma
4.1
(noting
\mathrm{A}_{1}+\mathrm{A}_{2}=\mathrm{A}_{12}
) that

\begin{split}&P_{a}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\mathrm{A}}}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{A}_{1}}{\mathrm{A}}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{1},\ \frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{A}_{12}}{\mathrm{A}}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{2}\right],\\
&P_{b}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\mathrm{A}}}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{A}_{12}}{\mathrm{A}}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{2}\right].\end{split}

For
P_{c}
, we first move the contour for
v_{12}
to the right of the contour for
v_{23}
. This can be done without changing the value of the integral since the integrand is analytic at
v_{12}=v_{23}
.
We then change all variables
v_{*}\mapsto-v_{*}
. Reversing the orientation of the contour for
v_{0}
, we find that

\begin{split}&P_{c}=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{0}\int_{-2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{23}\int_{-3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{12}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}_{2}v^{2}_{0}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{23}v^{2}_{23}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}v_{23}}}{(v_{23}-v_{0})(v_{0}-v_{12})}\end{split}

where all contours are oriented upwards.
Moving the
v_{0}
-contour across the
v_{23}
-contour to the left, and taking into account the simple pole
v_{0}=v_{23}
, we find that

\begin{split}P_{c}=&\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{3}}\int_{-2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{0}\int_{\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{23}\int_{-3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{12}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{B}_{2}v^{2}_{0}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}(\mathrm{r}_{2}-\mathrm{r}_{1})v_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{23}v^{2}_{23}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{1}v_{23}}}{(v_{23}-v_{0})(v_{0}-v_{12})}\\
&-\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{-2+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{23}\int_{-3+\mathrm{i}{\mathbb{R}}}\text{d}v_{12}\frac{e^{\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{A}_{12}v^{2}_{12}+\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{12}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{B}_{2}+\mathrm{A}_{23})v^{2}_{23}-\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathrm{r}_{2}v_{23}}}{v_{23}-v_{12}}.\end{split}

Noting that
\mathrm{A}_{12}+\mathrm{B}_{2}=\mathrm{A}_{1}
and
\mathrm{B}_{2}+\mathrm{A}_{23}=\mathrm{A}_{3}
, Lemma
4.1
implies that

\begin{split}\sqrt{2\pi\mathrm{A}}P_{c}=\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{A}_{12}}{\mathrm{A}}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{2},\ \frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{A}_{1}}{\mathrm{A}}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{1}\right]-\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{A}_{12}}{\mathrm{A}}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{2}\right].\end{split}

We have
\sqrt{\mathrm{A}}=\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}\mathsf{c}_{+}
, and, from (
7.29
),
\frac{\mathrm{A}_{1}}{\mathrm{A}}=\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{1}-x_{1}}{\mathrm{m}-1}
and
\frac{\mathrm{A}_{12}}{\mathrm{A}}=\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{2}-x_{2}}{\mathrm{m}-1}
.
The result now follows since
\mathsf{\sigma}^{2}\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathsf{c}_{-}=\frac{\ell\sqrt{D}}{2\sqrt{ab}}
.
∎

7.5
Estimation of the remainder and the proof of the theorem

In this section, we state estimates for the remaining integrals and use them to complete the proof of Theorem
1.5
.
The estimates are given in two propositions, each applying to different choices of
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
.
The first proposition implies estimates for
\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, and for both
\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}
and
\mathsf{Q}^{(1,2,1)}_{L}
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
The second proposition gives estimates on the remaining cases of
\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}
.
The proof of Theorem
1.5
is given at the end of this subsection.

Recall that the integrals
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
depend on
L>0
.
Recall also the constant
\mathsf{Z}_{L}
from (
7.39
). The proof of the following proposition is given in Subsection
7.6
.

Proposition 7.11
.

For every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, there exist constants
C,c,L_{0}>0
such that for every
L\geq L_{0}
and
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
,
and for every
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
of the forms

1.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=2^{a_{2}}(23)^{a_{23}}3^{a_{3}}(123)^{a_{123}}(12)^{a_{12}}1^{a_{1}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime}}1^{b_{1}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
;

2.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{a_{3}}(23)^{a_{23}}2^{a_{2}}(123)^{a_{123}}(12)^{a_{12}}1^{a_{1}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime}}1^{b_{1}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
;

3.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{a_{3}}(23)^{a_{23}}(123)^{a_{123}}1^{a_{1}}(12)^{a_{12}}2^{a_{2}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime}}1^{b_{1}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
;

4.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{a_{3}}(23)^{a_{23}}(123)^{a_{123}}1^{a_{1}}(12)^{a_{12}}2^{a_{2}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=2^{b_{2}}(23)^{b_{23}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}(12)^{b_{12}}1^{b_{1}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
;

satisfying

•

(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\neq(1,0,0,0,0,0)
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
,

•

a_{123}+a_{1}\geq 1
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

•

(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},b_{2},a_{3})\neq(1,0,0,0,0,0)
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

we have

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq C^{|\mathbf{n}|}\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1})!(n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3})!n_{3}!}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L},

(7.43)

where
b_{3}=b_{3}^{\prime}+b_{3}^{\prime\prime}
.

Together with Lemma
7.8
, Lemma
7.9
, Proposition
7.10
, the above result implies the following.

Corollary 7.12
.

As
L\to\infty
, the following hold:

\begin{split}&\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}=\frac{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}{2\pi LD}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{i}-x_{i}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{i},i=1,2\right](1+o(1))\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}$,}\\
&\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}+\frac{1}{4}\mathsf{Q}^{(1,2,1)}_{L}=\frac{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}{2\pi LD}\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{i}-x_{i}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{i},i=1,2\right](1+o(1))\quad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$.}\end{split}

Proof.

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
, we use Lemma
7.8
(a) to see that

\mathsf{Q}^{(1,1,1)}_{L}=-\mathrm{I}^{231}_{123}-\mathrm{I}^{3(12)}_{123}-\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
.
The integrals
\mathrm{I}^{231}_{123}
and
\mathrm{I}^{3(12)}_{123}
are of the forms in Proposition
7.11
(a). Thus, comparing the estimate (
7.43
) with the asymptotics of
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{123}
evaluated in Proposition
7.10
, we obtain the result for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
.

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
, we use Lemma
7.8
(b), Proposition
7.11
(b), and Proposition
7.10
.

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
, we use Lemma
7.8
(c), Proposition
7.11
(c), and Proposition
7.10
.

For
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, we use Lemma
7.8
(d), Lemma
7.9
, Proposition
7.11
(d), and Proposition
7.10
. Here we note that
(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},b_{2},a_{3})=(1,0,0,0,0,0)
for
the integral
\mathrm{I}^{(123)}_{(12)3}
from Lemma
7.8
(d), as well as for the integral
\mathrm{I}^{(123)2}_{(23)(12)}
from Lemma
7.9
. Thus, Proposition
7.11
(d) does not apply to these integrals; they are instead evaluated in Proposition
7.10
.
∎

The next proposition is proved in Subsection
7.7
. It will be used to estimate the remainder of the series (
7.7
).

Proposition 7.13
.

For every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, there exist constants
C,c,L_{0}>0
such that for every
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
with
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1)
, and for
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
of the forms

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}1^{n_{1}}2^{n_{22}}3^{n_{32}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{n^{\prime}_{31}}2^{n^{\prime}_{21}}1^{n_{1}}2^{n^{\prime}_{22}}3^{n^{\prime}_{32}}

(7.44)

satisfying

•

\mathbf{n}\neq(1,2,1)
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

•

n_{1}\geq n_{21}+1
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

we have, for every
L\geq L_{0}
,

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq C^{|\mathbf{n}|}\frac{(n_{1}!)^{3/2}(n_{2}!)^{2}(n_{3}!)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{(n_{1}\vee n_{2}-n_{1}\wedge n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3}-n_{2}\wedge n_{3})!}}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}.

(7.45)

To estimate the series (
7.7
) using the above result, we also need the following lemma.

Lemma 7.14
.

For every
A>0
, the following series is convergent:

\sum_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}=1}^{\infty}\frac{A^{n_{1}+n_{2}+n_{3}}}{\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{1}\vee n_{2}-n_{1}\wedge n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3}-n_{2}\wedge n_{3})!n_{3}!}}.

(7.46)

Proof.

Using the inequality
\frac{M!(N-M)!}{N!}\geq\frac{1}{2^{N}}
, we find that

a!(a\vee b-a\wedge b)!\geq a!(a\vee b-a)!\geq\frac{(a\vee b)!}{2^{a\vee b}}
for all positive integers
a
and
b
.
Thus,

n_{1}!(n_{1}\vee n_{2}-n_{1}\wedge n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3}-n_{2}\wedge n_{3})!n_{3}!\geq\frac{(n_{1}\vee n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3})!}{2^{n_{1}\vee n_{2}+n_{2}\vee n_{3}}}\geq\frac{(n_{1}\vee n_{2}\vee n_{3})!}{2^{2(n_{1}\vee n_{2}\vee n_{3})}}.

Hence, the series is dominated by

\sum_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}=1}^{\infty}\frac{(2A^{3})^{n_{1}\vee n_{2}\vee n_{3}}}{\sqrt{(n_{1}\vee n_{2}\vee n_{3})!}}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(2A^{3})^{n}}{\sqrt{n!}}(n^{3}-(n-1)^{3}).

(7.47)

The last series is convergent.
∎

We now obtain an estimate for the remainder of the series (
7.7
).

Corollary 7.15
.

For every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that, as
L\to\infty
,

\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\setminus\{(1,1,1)\}}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{n}!)^{2}}|\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}|=O\left(e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\right)\qquad\text{if $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}$,}

and

\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\setminus\{(1,1,1),(1,2,1)\}}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{n}!)^{2}}|\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}|=O\left(e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\right)\qquad\text{if $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$.}

Proof.

We use the formula for
\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}
given in Lemma
7.4
.
We take the
z_{i}
-contours in sum to be circles of fixed radii larger than
1
; for concreteness, we choose them to be the circles of radii
2
centered at the origin. Since

\left|\oint_{|z|=2}\frac{(z+1)^{n-n^{\prime}-1}}{z^{n^{\prime}-i+1}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\right|\leq\frac{3^{n}}{2^{n^{\prime}-i}}\leq 3^{n+n^{\prime}}

for
0\leq i\leq 2n^{\prime}
, we find that, for each
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
,

|\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}|\leq 3^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\sum_{i=0\vee(2n_{2}-n_{1}+1)}^{2n_{2}}\sum_{j=0\vee(2n_{3}-n_{2}+1)}^{2n_{3}}|\alpha_{ij}|,

(7.48)

where
\alpha_{ij}
is a sum of
\binom{2n_{2}}{i}\binom{2n_{3}}{j}
terms, each of the form
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
, with
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
of the forms indicated in Lemma
7.4
.
Since
i\geq 2n_{2}-n_{1}+1
in the sum, we find that
n_{22}+n_{22}^{\prime}=i\geq 2n_{2}-n_{1}+1
, which implies
n_{1}\geq n_{21}+n_{21}^{\prime}+1
.
Hence,
n_{1}\geq n_{21}+1
, which is one of the conditions of Proposition
7.13
.
Using
\binom{2n_{2}}{i}\binom{2n_{3}}{j}\leq 2^{2n_{2}+2n_{3}}\leq 2^{2|\mathbf{n}|}
and applying Proposition
7.13
,
(
7.48
) implies that for every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, there exist constants
C,c,L_{0}>0
such that

|\mathsf{Q}^{(\mathbf{n})}_{L}|\leq 4n_{2}n_{3}3^{2|\mathbf{n}|}2^{2|\mathbf{n}|}C^{|\mathbf{n}|}\frac{(n_{1}!)^{3/2}(n_{2}!)^{2}(n_{3}!)^{3/2}}{\sqrt{(n_{1}\vee n_{2}-n_{1}\wedge n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3}-n_{2}\wedge n_{3})!}}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}

(7.49)

for every
L\geq L_{0}
,
for every
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\setminus\{(1,1,1)\}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, and for every
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\setminus\{(1,1,1),(1,2,1)\}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Thus, setting
A=48C
, we have

\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\setminus\{(1,1,1)\}}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{n}!)^{2}}|\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}|\leq e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\setminus\{(1,1,1)\}}\frac{A^{|\mathbf{n}|}}{\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{1}\vee n_{2}-n_{1}\wedge n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3}-n_{2}\wedge n_{3})!n_{3}!}}

for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, and

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\\
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1),(1,2,1)\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{(\mathbf{n}!)^{2}}|\mathsf{Q}_{L}^{(\mathbf{n})}|\leq e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}\\
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1),(1,2,1)\end{subarray}}\frac{A^{|\mathbf{n}|}}{\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{1}\vee n_{2}-n_{1}\wedge n_{2})!(n_{2}\vee n_{3}-n_{2}\wedge n_{3})!n_{3}!}}

for
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
. The series on the right is convergent converges due to Lemma
7.14
, and we obtain the result.
∎

We now complete the proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

Suppose that (see (
7.1
))
\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1

and
h(x_{1},y_{1})<h(x_{2},y_{2})
, i.e.
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}
(see (
7.32
)).
Then, Corollary
7.12
and Corollary
7.15
, together with (
7.7
), imply that for every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

\begin{split}\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{2\pi LD}{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\mathsf{Q}_{3}(\mathbf{M}_{L},\mathbf{N}_{L},\mathbf{T}_{L})=\mathbb{P}\left[\mathsf{c}_{+}\mathbb{B}^{\mathrm{br}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}y_{i}-x_{i}}{\mathrm{m}-1}\right)>\mathrm{r}_{i},i=1,2\right].\end{split}

(7.50)

The analysis for
\mathsf{Q}_{1}(aL,bL,\ell L)
is similar (and easier), and we find

\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{2\pi LD}{\sqrt{ab}\mathsf{Z}_{L}}\mathsf{Q}_{1}(aL,bL,\ell L)=1
.
Thus, we obtain (
7.6
), proving Theorem
1.5
in this case.

Now consider
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}\setminus(\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7})
.
In this situation,
\mathsf{q}
lies on the boundary of two sub-regions
\mathsf{R}_{i}
and
\mathsf{R}_{i+1}
for some
i=1,\cdots,6
.
The boundary between
\mathsf{R}_{i}
and
\mathsf{R}_{i+1}
is a subset of the hypersurface
\{(x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2})\in(0,1)^{4}:g(x_{1},y_{1})=g(x_{2},y_{2})\}
, where
g(x,y)
equals
x
,
\frac{1-y}{1-x}
,
y-x
,
\frac{y}{x}
,
\mathrm{m}y-x
, and
y
for
i=1,\cdots,6
, respectively.
Note that the right-hand side of (
7.50
) is continuous in
x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2}
.
Hence, by applying Lemma
4.2
(where
y
in the lemma is either
x_{1}
or
y_{1}
, depending on the regime), we find that Theorem
1.5
also holds for
