\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}\setminus(\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7})
.
Therefore, we have now proved Theorem
1.5
when
\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1
and
h(x_{1},y_{1})<h(x_{2},y_{2})
.

If
h(x_{1},y_{1})>h(x_{2},y_{2})
, the result follows by relabeling the points, since the limit is invariant under interchanging
(x_{1},y_{1})
and
(x_{2},y_{2})
.
If
h(x_{1},y_{1})=h(x_{2},y_{2})
, the result again follows from Lemma
4.2
.
Thus, Theorem
1.5
is proved when
\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1
.

Now suppose that
1<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<\mathrm{m}
.
Since
\mathcal{L}(m,n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\mathcal{L}(n,m)
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\mathcal{L}(x_{i}aN,y_{i}bN)-h(x_{i},y_{i})N}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}N^{1/2}}>\mathrm{r}_{i},\ i=1,2\bigg|\mathcal{L}(aN,bN)=\ell N\right]\end{split}

is equal to

\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{\mathcal{L}(y_{i}bN,x_{i}aN)-h(x_{i},y_{i})N}{\sqrt{2}\mathsf{\sigma}N^{1/2}}>\mathrm{r}_{i},\ i=1,2\bigg|\mathcal{L}(bN,aN)=\ell N\right].

We observe that
D
in (
1.7
) and
\mathsf{\sigma}
in (
1.14
) are symmetric with respect to
a
and
b
.
The function
h(x,y)
in (
1.10
), which involves
a
and
b
, is invariant under simultaneous exchange of
a\leftrightarrow b
and
x\leftrightarrow y
.
Finally,
\mathsf{c}_{\pm}
in (
1.14
) become
\mathsf{c}_{\mp}
when
a
and
b
are swapped.
From these observations, the part of Theorem
1.5
for
1<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<\mathrm{m}
follows from the case
\frac{1}{\mathrm{m}}<\frac{y_{1}}{x_{1}},\frac{y_{2}}{x_{2}}<1
.
This completes the proof.
∎

7.6
Bounds of the integrals and proof of Proposition
7.11

We estimate the integrals appearing in Proposition
7.11
. From (
7.24
), the integrals are of the form

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{|{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}|}}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}\,\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}).

(7.51)

We note that if
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
and
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})=\mathbf{a}=(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})
, then

n_{1}=a_{1}+a_{12}+a_{123},\quad n_{2}=a_{2}+a_{12}+a_{23}+a_{123},\quad n_{3}=a_{3}+a_{23}+a_{123},

(7.52)

and thus,

|\mathbf{a}|=a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{12}+a_{23}+a_{123}\leq|\mathbf{n}|\leq 3|\mathbf{a}|\quad\text{and}\quad n_{2}+a_{1}+a_{3}=|\mathbf{a}|

(7.53)

The rational function
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
satisfies the following estimate. Note that the well-known bound
N!\geq N^{N}e^{-N}
implies that
N^{N}\leq e^{N}N!\leq 4^{N}N!
for every positive integer
N
.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
and
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
.
Set
\mathbf{a}=\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}})
and
\mathbf{b}=\type({\boldsymbol{\tau}})
.
Suppose that
\gamma_{*}
and
\Gamma_{*}
for
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
are twelve contours, all contained in the disk of radius
2
centered at the origin, and that every pair is separated by a distance of at least
d>0
.
Then,

|\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})|\leq\frac{2^{4|\mathbf{n}|}}{d^{|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{b}|}}\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1})!(n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3})!n_{3}!}

(7.54)

for every
\boldsymbol{\xi}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}^{123},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{12},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{23},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{3})
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}=(\boldsymbol{\eta}^{123},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{12},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{2},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{3})
satisfying
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{*}\in(\gamma_{*})^{a_{*}}
and
\boldsymbol{\eta}^{*}\in(\Gamma_{*})^{b_{*}}
for each
*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}
.

Proof.

By the definition (
7.22
),
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
is the product of four Cauchy determinants of sizes
n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1},n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3},n_{3}
, respectively, and the polynomial
\mathrm{S}_{n_{3}}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{123},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{23},\boldsymbol{\xi}_{3}|\boldsymbol{\eta}_{123},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{23},\boldsymbol{\eta}_{3})
is given by (
3.4
).
Hadamard’s inequality implies that

|\mathrm{K}_{n}(\mathsf{r}|\mathsf{s})|\leq\prod_{j=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r_{i}-s_{j})^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\leq\frac{n^{n/2}}{(\min_{i,j}|r_{i}-s_{j}|)^{n}}\leq\frac{2^{n}\sqrt{n!}}{(\min_{i,j}|r_{i}-s_{j}|)^{n}}.

On the other hand,
|\mathrm{S}_{n}(\mathbf{r}|\mathbf{s})|\leq n\max_{i=1}^{n}|r_{i}-s_{i}|
. Thus, we obtain

|\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})|\leq 4n_{3}\frac{2^{2n_{2}+a_{1}+b_{1}+a_{3}+b_{3}}\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1})!(n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3})!n_{3}!}}{d^{2n_{2}+a_{1}+b_{1}+a_{3}+b_{3}}}

for
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contour.
From (
7.53
), we have
2n_{2}+a_{1}+b_{1}+a_{3}+b_{3}=|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{b}|\leq 2|\mathbf{n}|
.
Furthermore, since
4n_{3}\leq 4^{n_{3}}\leq 2^{2|\mathbf{n}|}
, we obtain the result.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

For every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, define the constants

\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}=\mathrm{G}_{*}(z_{*}^{+})-\mathrm{G}_{*}(z_{*}^{-}),\qquad*\in\{1,2,3,12,23,123\}.

(7.55)

There exist constants
C,c,L_{0}>0
such that for every
L\geq L_{0}
and
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
,

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq C^{|\mathbf{n}|}\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1})!(n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3})!n_{3}!}\,L^{3|{\mathbf{v}}|}e^{c|{\mathbf{v}}|L^{1/2}}e^{-L\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}}

(7.56)

for every
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
of the following forms:

1.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=2^{a_{2}}(23)^{a_{23}}3^{a_{3}}(123)^{a_{123}}(12)^{a_{12}}1^{a_{1}}
and

{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime}}1^{b_{1}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}

if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
;

2.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{a_{3}}(23)^{a_{23}}2^{a_{2}}(123)^{a_{123}}(12)^{a_{12}}1^{a_{1}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime}}1^{b_{1}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
;

3.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{a_{3}}(23)^{a_{23}}(123)^{a_{123}}1^{a_{1}}(12)^{a_{12}}2^{a_{2}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime}}1^{b_{1}}2^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
;

4.

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{a_{3}}(23)^{a_{23}}(123)^{a_{123}}1^{a_{1}}(12)^{a_{12}}2^{a_{2}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=2^{b_{2}}(23)^{b_{23}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime}}(12)^{b_{12}}1^{b_{1}}3^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

where
{\mathbf{v}}=(v_{123},v_{12},v_{23},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})
is given by

{\mathbf{v}}=\begin{cases}(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}$,}\\
(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},b_{2},a_{3})\qquad\text{for $\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}$.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
and
a_{2}>0
in (a), then
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=0
. Similarly, if
w\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
and
b_{2}>0
in (d), then
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=0
.

Proof.

Suppose that
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
and
a_{2}>0
. Since
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, (
7.35
) implies that
x_{2}-x_{1}<0
and
y_{2}-y_{1}>0
.
Thus, recalling (
7.5
),
M_{L,2}-M_{L,1}=\lceil x_{2}aL\rceil-\lceil x_{1}aL\rceil
is a non-positive integer
and
N_{L,2}-N_{L,1}=\lceil y_{2}aL\rceil-\lceil y_{1}aL\rceil
is a non-negative integer.
Therefore,

f_{L,2}(z)=\frac{z^{N_{L,2}-N_{L,1}}e^{(T_{L,2}-T_{L,1})z}}{(1+z)^{M_{L,2}-M_{L,1}}}

is analytic at
z=-1
. Hence, the function
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
is analytic at
\xi^{2}_{i}=-1
.
Since the
\xi^{2}_{i}
-contour is the innermost among all
\boldsymbol{\xi}
-contours, it follows by Cauchy’s theorem that

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{\tau}=0
.

Similarly, suppose that
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
and
b_{2}>0
.
Since
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
, (
7.35
) implies that
x_{2}-x_{1}>0
and
y_{2}-y_{1}<0
.
Thus, in this case,
\frac{1}{f_{L,2}(z)}
is analytic at
z=0
, so
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
is analytic at
\eta^{2}_{i}=0
.
Again, the
\eta^{2}_{i}
-contour is the innermost among all
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-contours, and thus
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{\tau}=0
by Cauchy’s theorem.

In what follows, we assume that
a_{2}=0
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
and
b_{2}=0
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

(i) Let
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}
and consider the integral
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are as in (a).
From Lemma
7.6
, the critical points satisfy

-1<z^{-}_{2}<z^{-}_{23}<z^{-}_{3}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{1}<z_{1}^{+}=z^{+}_{2}=z^{+}_{3}=\mathsf{z}_{c}<0.

For each
*
, we take the
\xi_{i}^{*}
-contour to be the circle
\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z+1|=|1+z^{-}_{*}|\}
.
On the other hand, we take the contours for the
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-variables to be

(\Sigma_{1,L})^{b_{3}^{\prime}}\times(\Sigma_{2,L})^{b_{2}^{\prime}}\times(\Sigma_{3,L})^{b_{1}}\times(\Sigma_{4,L})^{b_{2}^{\prime\prime}}\times(\Sigma_{5,L})^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}},

where
\Sigma_{k,L}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z|=|\mathsf{z}_{c}|-(6-k)L^{-1/2}\}
.
We may choose these contours as above without changing the value of the integral, since
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are of the forms specified in (a).

Note that all circles are contained in the disk of radius
2
centered at the origin, and each pair is separated by
L^{1/2}
, for all sufficiently large
L
.
From Lemma
7.16
, with
d=L^{-1/2}
, and using
|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{b}|\leq|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{n}|\leq 4|\mathbf{a}|
from (
7.53
),
we find that

|\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})|\leq 2^{4|\mathbf{n}|}L^{2|\mathbf{a}|}\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1})!(n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3})!n_{3}!}

(7.57)

uniformly for every
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contour, for all sufficiently large
L
.

On the other hand, from Lemmas
7.7
and
5.3
,
also using
|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{b}|\leq 2|\mathbf{n}|
, there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
\mathbf{n}
and
L
, such that

\left|{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|=\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{a_{*}}\left|f_{L,*}(\xi_{i}^{*})\right|}{\prod_{i=1}^{b_{*}}\left|f_{L,*}(\eta_{i}^{*})\right|}\leq C^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\frac{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})\right|^{a_{*}}}{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{+}_{*})\right|^{b_{*}}}

(7.58)

uniformly for every
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contour, for all sufficiently large
L
.
From (
7.12
) and the relation (
7.52
), we find that

\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}|f_{L,*}(z)|^{a_{*}}=\prod_{i=1}^{3}|f_{L,i}(z)|^{n_{i}}=\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}|f_{L,*}(z)|^{b_{*}}\qquad\text{for every $z$.}

(7.59)

Thus, since
z_{*}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}
for all
*
, we have

\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\frac{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})\right|^{a_{*}}}{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{+}_{*})\right|^{b_{*}}}=\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\frac{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})\right|^{a_{*}}}{\left|f_{L,*}(\mathsf{z}_{c})\right|^{a_{*}}}=\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\left|\frac{f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})}{f_{L,*}(z^{+}_{*})}\right|^{a_{*}}.

Hence, from the formula (
7.13
), we find that there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\left|{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|\leq C^{2|\mathbf{n}|}e^{c|\mathbf{a}|L^{1/2}}e^{-L\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}a_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}}

(7.60)

uniformly for every
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contour, for all sufficiently large
L
.
Applying estimates (
7.57
) and (
7.60
) to (
7.51
) yields (
7.56
), possibly after adjusting the constants.

(ii) When
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, the analysis is the same as in (i), except that we do not have the
\boldsymbol{\xi}^{2}
-integrals since we assumed that
a_{2}=0
.

(iii) When
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
or
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
, the proof is nearly the same as in (i). We omit the details.

(iv) Let
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}
. The analysis is again similar, but with some modifications, since in this case the critical points satisfy

-1<z^{-}_{3}<z^{-}_{23}<z_{123}^{-}<z^{-}_{1}<z^{-}_{12}<z^{-}_{2}=z^{+}_{1}=z^{+}_{3}=z^{+}_{12}=z^{+}_{23}=\mathsf{z}_{c}<z^{+}_{2}<0.

Now
z_{2}^{-}=\mathsf{z}_{c}<z_{2}^{+}
, unlike in cases (i)–(iii).
For every
*
, we take the
\xi_{i}^{*}
-contour to be the circle
\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z+1|=|1+z^{-}_{*}|\}
.
On the other hand, we take the contours for the
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-variables to be

\Sigma^{b_{2}}\times(\Sigma_{1,L})^{b_{23}}\times(\Sigma_{2,L})^{b_{3}^{\prime}}\times(\Sigma_{3,L})^{b_{12}}\times(\Sigma_{4,L})^{b_{1}}\times(\Sigma_{5,L})^{b_{3}^{\prime\prime}}

where
\Sigma=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z|=|z^{+}_{2}|\}

and
\Sigma_{k,L}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,:\,|z|=|\mathsf{z}_{c}|-(6-k)L^{-1/2}\}
.

From (
7.52
), we see that

a_{2}\leq n_{2}=b_{2}+b_{12}+b_{23}+b_{123}
and

b_{1}+b_{3}+2(b_{12}+b_{23}+b_{123})\leq 2(n_{1}+n_{3})=2(a_{1}+a_{3}+a_{12}+a_{23}+2a_{123})\leq 4(|\mathbf{a}|-a_{2})
.
Thus,

|\mathbf{a}|+|\mathbf{b}|\leq(|\mathbf{a}|-a_{2})+2b_{2}+b_{1}+b_{3}+2(b_{12}+b_{23}+b_{123})\leq 2b_{2}+5(|\mathbf{a}|-a_{2})\leq 6(|\mathbf{a}|-a_{2}+b_{2}).

Hence, Lemma
7.16
with
d=L^{-1/2}
implies that

|\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})|\leq 2^{4|\mathbf{n}|}L^{3(|\mathbf{a}|-a_{2}+b_{2})}\sqrt{n_{1}!(n_{2}-n_{1}+a_{1}+b_{1})!(n_{2}-n_{3}+a_{3}+b_{3})!n_{3}!}

uniformly for every
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contour, for all sufficiently large
L
.

From Lemmas
7.7
and
5.3
, the estimate (
7.58
) still hold.
Since
z_{2}^{-}=z^{+}_{1}=z^{+}_{3}=z^{+}_{12}=z^{+}_{23}=\mathsf{z}_{c}
, the identity (
7.59
) implies that

\prod_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}\frac{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})\right|^{a_{*}}}{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{+}_{*})\right|^{b_{*}}}=\frac{|f_{L,2}(\mathsf{z}_{c})|^{b_{2}}}{|f_{L,2}(z_{2}^{+})|^{b_{2}}}\prod_{*\neq 2}\frac{\left|f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})\right|^{a_{*}}}{\left|f_{L,*}(\mathsf{z}_{c})\right|^{a_{*}}}=\frac{|f_{L,2}(z_{2}^{-})|^{b_{2}}}{|f_{L,2}(z_{2}^{+})|^{b_{2}}}\prod_{*\neq 2}\left|\frac{f_{L,*}(z^{-}_{*})}{f_{L,*}(z^{+}_{*})}\right|^{a_{*}}.

Hence, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\left|{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})\right|\leq C^{2|\mathbf{n}|}e^{c(|\mathbf{a}|-a_{2}+b_{2})L^{1/2}}e^{-L(b_{2}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{2}+\sum_{*\neq 2}a_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*})}

uniformly for
(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
on the contour, for all sufficiently large
L
.
Hence, (
7.56
) follows, after adjusting the constants if necessary.

(v) When
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
, the analysis is the same as in (iv), except that there are no
\eta^{2}_{i}
-integrals since we have assumed that
b_{2}=0
.
∎

We now estimate the terms
\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}
appearing in (
7.56
).
This estimate is provided in Lemma
7.20
below, which makes use of the following two lemmas.

Lemma 7.18
.

1.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then for every
*\in\{1,3,12,23,123\}
, the function

\mathrm{G}_{*}
is strictly decreasing on
(-1,z_{*}^{-}]
, strictly increasing on
[z_{*}^{-},z_{*}^{+}]
, and strictly decreasing on
[z_{*}^{+},0)
.

2.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
, then
\mathrm{G}_{2}
is strictly decreasing on
(-1,z_{2}^{-}]
, strictly increasing on
[z_{2}^{-},z_{2}^{+}]
, and strictly decreasing on
[z_{2}^{+},0)
.

3.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, then
\mathrm{G}_{2}
is strictly increasing on
(-1,z_{2}^{+}]
and strictly decreasing on
[z_{2}^{+},0)
.

4.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then
\mathrm{G}_{2}
is strictly decreasing on
(-1,z_{2}^{-}]
and strictly increasing on
[z_{2}^{-},0)
.

Proof.

The proof follows from Lemma
7.5
, since
\mathrm{G}^{\prime}_{*}(z)=\frac{q_{*}(z)}{z(z+1)}
for a convex quadratic polynomial
q_{*}
.
∎

Lemma 7.19
.

For every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, there exists a constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that the following statements hold:

1.

\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{2}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}

if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
.

2.

\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

3.

\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

4.

\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

5.

\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
.

Proof.

1.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
.
By definition,
\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{2}+\mathrm{G}_{3}=\mathrm{G}_{123}
.
If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, then
z_{*}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}
for all
*\in\{1,2,3,123\}
by Lemma
7.6
, and thus,
\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{3}^{+})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(\mathsf{z}_{c})
.
If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}
, then by Lemma
7.18
(b),
\mathrm{G}_{2}(\mathsf{z}_{c})=\mathrm{G}_{2}(z^{-}_{2})<\mathrm{G}_{2}(z^{+}_{2})
, and thus,

\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{3}^{+})>\mathrm{G}_{1}(\mathsf{z}_{c})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(\mathsf{z}_{c})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(\mathsf{z}_{c})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(\mathsf{z}_{c})
.
In either case,
\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{2}^{+})\geq\mathrm{G}_{123}(z_{123}^{+})
.
On the other hand, since
z_{123}^{-}\in(-1,z_{*}^{+})\setminus\{z_{*}^{-}\}

for each
*=1,2,3
by Lemma

7.6
, Lemma
7.18
(a) and (b) imply that
\mathrm{G}_{*}(z_{123}^{-})>\mathrm{G}_{*}(z_{*}^{-})
for
*=1,2,3
. Thus,
\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{3}^{-})<\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{123}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{123}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{123}^{-})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(z_{123}^{-})
. Therefore, we find that
\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{2}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}>\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}
. This implies the result.

2.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
. The result follows by noting that
\mathrm{G}_{12}+\mathrm{G}_{3}=\mathrm{G}_{123}
, that
z_{*}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}
for all
*\in\{12,3,123\}
, and that
z_{123}^{-}\in(-1,z_{*}^{+})\setminus\{z_{*}^{-}\}
for
*\in\{12,3\}
.

3.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
. The result follows by noting that
\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{23}=\mathrm{G}_{123}
, that
z_{*}^{+}=\mathsf{z}_{c}
for all
*\in\{1,23,123\}
, and that
z_{123}^{-}\in(-1,z_{*}^{+})\setminus\{z_{*}^{-}\}
for
*\in\{1,23\}
.

4.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
. Note that
\mathrm{G}_{1}+\mathrm{G}_{3}=\mathrm{G}_{123}-\mathrm{G}_{2}
.
Since
z_{1}^{+}=z_{3}^{+}=z_{123}^{+}=z_{2}^{-}
, we have

\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{3}^{+})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(z_{123}^{+})-\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{-})
.
On the other hand, since
z_{123}^{-}\in(-1,z_{*}^{+})\setminus\{z_{*}^{-}\}
for
*=1,3
, Lemma
7.18
(a) implies that

\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{3}^{-})<\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{123}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{3}(z_{123}^{-})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(z_{123}^{-})-\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{123}^{-})
.
Hence,
\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}>\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{123}^{-})-\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{-})
.
Since
z_{123}^{-}\in(-1,z_{2}^{-})
, Lemma
7.18
(b) and (d) imply that
\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{123}^{-})>\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{-})
.
Thus, we obtain the result.

5.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
. The proof is trickier in this case.
Since
\mathrm{G}_{12}+\mathrm{G}_{23}=\mathrm{G}_{123}+\mathrm{G}_{2}
and
z_{*}^{+}
are equal for all
*
,
\mathrm{G}_{12}(z_{12}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{23}(z_{23}^{+})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(z_{123}^{+})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{+})
.
On the other hand, since
z_{123}^{-}\in(z_{23}^{-},z_{23}^{+})
, Lemma
7.18
(a) implies that
\mathrm{G}_{23}(z_{23}^{-})<\mathrm{G}_{23}(z_{123}^{-})
.
Furthermore, noting that
z^{-}_{1}\in(z_{12}^{-},z^{+}_{12})
, we find from Lemma
7.18
(a) that

\mathrm{G}_{12}(z_{12}^{-})<\mathrm{G}_{12}(z_{1}^{-})=\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{1}^{-})
.
Since
z_{123}^{-}\in(-1,z_{1}^{-})
, applying Lemma
7.18
(a) again, we have
\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{1}^{-})<\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{123}^{-})
.
Hence,
\mathrm{G}_{12}(z_{12}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{23}(z_{23}^{-})<\mathrm{G}_{1}(z_{123}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{1}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{23}(z_{123}^{-})=\mathrm{G}_{123}(z_{123}^{-})+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{1}^{-})
.
Thus,
\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23}>\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{+})-\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{1}^{-})
.
Since
z_{1}^{-}\in(\max\{-1,z_{2}^{-}\},z_{2}^{+})
, Lemma
7.18
(a) and (c) imply that
\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{2}^{+})>\mathrm{G}_{2}(z_{1}^{-})
. We thus obtain the result.

∎

We now estimate
\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}
. We note that, for positive integers
p
and
q
,

if
p\geq q
, then
p-q+1\geq\frac{p}{q}
.

(7.61)

Lemma 7.20
.

For every
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+c|{\mathbf{v}}|

(7.62)

for every

{\mathbf{v}}=(v_{123},v_{12},v_{23},v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{6}\setminus\{(1,0,0,0,0,0)\}
satisfying

v_{1}+v_{12}+v_{123}\geq 1,\qquad v_{3}+v_{23}+v_{123}\geq 1

(7.63)

with the following extra assumptions:

•

v_{2}+v_{12}+v_{23}+v_{123}\geq 1
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
,

•

v_{2}=0
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
,

•

v_{123}+v_{1}\geq 1
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.

Proof.

Fix
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Let
\epsilon_{0}>0
be the constant from Lemma
7.19
. Define the constants

\begin{split}&c_{1}=\min\left\{\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{2},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}\right\},\qquad c_{2}=\min\{c_{1},\epsilon_{0}\},\\
&c_{1}^{\prime}=\min\left\{\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1},\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}\right\},\qquad c_{2}^{\prime}=\min\{c_{1}^{\prime},\epsilon_{0}\}.\end{split}

(7.64)

Lemma
7.18
(a) and (b) imply that
c_{1},c_{2}>0
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}
,
and
c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime}>0
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
Set
\textnormal{LHS}:=\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}
.

1.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{2}\cup\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
.

•

Suppose
v_{123}\geq 1
. Then
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-1)c_{1}
.
If
v_{123}\geq 2
, then
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 2
.
On the other hand, if
v_{123}=1
, then by the assumption that
{\mathbf{v}}\neq(1,0,0,0,0,0)
, there is at least one
*\neq 123
with
v_{*}\geq 1
.
Hence,
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 2
in this case as well.
Thus, from (
7.61
), we find that
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{1}
.

•

Suppose
v_{123}=0
.

(a)

Suppose
v_{12}\geq 1
and
v_{23}\geq 1
.
Then,
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}
by Lemma
7.19
(e). Since
\epsilon_{0}\geq c_{2}
and
c_{1}\geq c_{2}
, we find

\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-1)c_{2}
. Since
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq v_{12}+v_{23}\geq 2
, we conclude from (
7.61
) that
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
.

(b)

Suppose
v_{12}\geq 1
and
v_{23}=0
. Then the second inequality of (
7.63
) implies
v_{3}\geq 1
. Using Lemma
7.19
(b) and the fact that
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 2
, we obtain

\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{12}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-1)c_{2}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
.

(c)

Suppose
v_{12}=0
and
v_{23}\geq 1
. Then the first inequality of (
7.63
) implies
v_{1}\geq 1
. Thus, applying Lemma
7.19
(c), we again obtain
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
.

(d)

Suppose
v_{12}=v_{23}=0
. Then,
v_{1},v_{3}\geq 1
by (
7.63
). Additionally, from the condition
v_{2}+v_{12}+v_{23}+v_{123}\geq 1
, we find that
v_{2}\geq 1
. Thus, by Lemma
7.19
(a) and the fact that
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 3
, we obtain
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{2}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-3)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-3)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{2}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{3}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
.

2.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
. The proof of case (a) holds except for part (i), since Lemma
7.19
(e) is not applicable when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
. The part (i) is modified as follows.

•

Suppose
v_{123}=0
,
v_{12}\geq 1
, and
v_{23}\geq 1
. Since
v_{123}+v_{1}\geq 1
when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
, we find that
v_{1}\geq 1
. Thus, by Lemma
7.19
(c),
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-1)c_{2}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
, since
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 3\geq 2
.

3.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
.
The proof of case (a) again holds, with
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
replaced by
c_{1}^{\prime}
and
c_{2}^{\prime}
, respectively, with
v_{2}=0
, except for part (iv), since Lemma
7.19
(a) is not applicable when
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
.
However, part (iv) does not occur because
v_{2}=0
by assumption.

4.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}
. If
v_{123}\geq 1
, the result follows from the same proof as in case (a). On the other hand, suppose
v_{123}=0
. Then, by the assumption that
v_{123}+v_{1}\geq 1
, we have
v_{1}\geq 1
.

•

If
v_{123}=0
and
v_{23}\geq 1
, then,
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{23}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}
by Lemma
7.19
(c). Hence,

\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-1)c_{2}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
since
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 2
.

•

If
v_{123}=0
and
v_{23}=0
, then the second inequality of (
7.63
) implies
v_{3}\geq 1
.
Thus,
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{1}+\Delta\mathrm{G}_{3}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}
. By Lemma
7.19
(d),
\textnormal{LHS}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\epsilon_{0}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-2)c_{1}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+(|{\mathbf{v}}|-1)c_{2}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+\frac{1}{2}|{\mathbf{v}}|c_{2}
since
|{\mathbf{v}}|\geq 2
.

5.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
. The proof is exactly the same as case (d), with
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
replaced by
c_{1}^{\prime}
and
c_{2}^{\prime}
, respectively, ans with
v_{2}=0
.

∎

We now prove Proposition
7.11
.

Proof of Proposition
7.11
.

If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
and
a_{2}>0
, or if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
and
b_{2}>0
, then
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{\tau}=0
by Lemma
7.17
.
Thus, we may assume that
a_{2}=0
if
w\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, and
b_{2}=0
if
w\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
By Lemma
7.17
, it remains to estimate
L^{3|{\mathbf{v}}|}e^{c|{\mathbf{v}}|L^{1/2}}e^{-L\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}}
.
We verify that Lemma
7.20
applies.
We have
v_{1}=a_{1},v_{3}=a_{3},v_{12}=a_{12},v_{23}=a_{23},v_{123}=a_{123}
,
while
v_{2}=a_{2}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, and
v_{2}=b_{2}
if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
From (
7.52
), we see that
v_{1}+v_{12}+v_{123}=n_{1}\geq 1
and
v_{3}+v_{23}+v_{123}=n_{3}\geq 1
, and thus (
7.63
) is satisfied.
Furthermore, if
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathsf{R}_{5}
, then
v_{2}+v_{12}+v_{23}+v_{123}=n_{2}\geq 1
again by (
7.52
).
If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then
v_{2}=0
by our assumption that
a_{2}=0
if
w\in\mathsf{R}_{1}
, and
b_{2}=0
if
w\in\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
If
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}\cup\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
, then
v_{123}+v_{1}=a_{123}+a_{1}\geq 1
by assumption.
Therefore, all conditions of Lemma
7.20
are satisfied, and hence there exists
c^{\prime}>0
such that
\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}\geq\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}+c^{\prime}|{\mathbf{v}}|
.
Thus, there exists
L_{0}>0
such that

L^{3|{\mathbf{v}}|}e^{c|{\mathbf{v}}|L^{1/2}}e^{-L\sum_{*\in\mathcal{A}_{3}}v_{*}\Delta\mathrm{G}_{*}}\leq e^{-L\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}-|{\mathbf{v}}|(c^{\prime}L-cL^{1/2}-3\ln L)}\leq e^{-L\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}-\frac{1}{2}c^{\prime}|{\mathbf{v}}|L}

for every
L\geq L_{0}
.
Now, from the explicit formulas,
\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}=\mathsf{J}(\ell)
in the equation (
1.6
).
Comparing with the formula (
7.39
) of
\mathsf{Z}_{L}
, we see that
e^{-L\Delta\mathrm{G}_{123}}\leq\mathsf{Z}_{L}
.
Thus, we obtain the result.
∎

7.7
Deformation of Integrals and proof of Proposition
7.13

Unlike those in Proposition
7.11
, the integrals arising in Proposition
7.13
cannot be evaluated directly using the method of steepest descent, since the ordering of the critical points does not match the nesting of the contours.
To address this, we deform the contours and, after accounting for residues, rewrite these integrals as sums of integrals to which the method of steepest descent applies directly.
Because a residue term may produce integrals with critical points still incompatible with the contour nesting, this procedure must sometimes be repeated multiple times until all resulting integrals have compatible critical points and contour structures.
This reduction is accomplished by Lemmas
7.24
and
7.25
.
In this way, we express the integrals appearing in Proposition
7.13
as sums of those appearing in Proposition
7.11
. These lemmas also yield the proofs of Lemmas
7.8
and
7.9
.
The formal proof of Proposition
7.13
is given at the end of this subsection.

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
\Omega
be a region, and let
\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma_{2}
, and
\Gamma_{3}
be Jordan curves in
\Omega
that are nested from innermost
(\Gamma_{1})
to outermost
(\Gamma_{3})
, and can be continuously deformed into one another within
\Omega
. Let
F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})
be a meromorphic function with

•

simple poles at
u_{i}=v_{j}
for all
i
and
j
,

•

simple zeros at
u_{i}=u_{j}
and at
v_{i}=v_{j}
for all
i\neq j
,

•

symmetry in
u_{1},\ldots,u_{m}
, and separately in
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}
.

Then,

\begin{split}&\int_{\Gamma_{1}^{m}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{u}}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\,\,F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})=\sum_{i=0}^{m\wedge n}(-2\pi\mathrm{i})^{i}i!\binom{m}{i}\binom{n}{i}\int_{\Gamma_{3}^{m-i}}\mathrm{d}u_{i+1}\cdots\mathrm{d}u_{m}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\,\,\res_{u_{1}=v_{1},\cdots,u_{i}=v_{i}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}}).\end{split}

(7.65)

Proof.

The general case can be readily proved by induction on
m
. We omit the details and instead illustrate the case
m=2
with
n\geq 2
to show how the assumptions on
F
are used.
By moving the
{\mathbf{u}}
-contour outside the
{\mathbf{v}}
-contour, the Cauchy residue theorem implies

\begin{split}&\int_{\Gamma_{1}^{2}}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\,\,F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})=\int_{\Gamma_{3}^{2}}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\,\,F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})-2\pi\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\Gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\res_{u_{1}=v_{k}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})\\
&\qquad\qquad-2\pi\mathrm{i}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\int_{\Gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\res_{u_{2}=v_{k}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})+(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{n}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\res_{u_{1}=v_{k_{1}},u_{2}=v_{k_{2}}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}}).\end{split}

Observe that
\res_{u_{1}=v_{k},u_{2}=v_{k}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})=0
, since
F
has a simple zero at
u_{1}=u_{2}
and simple poles at
u_{1}=v_{k}
,
u_{2}=v_{k}
.
Thus, the last double sum is only over
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
.
By the symmetry of
F
in both
{\mathbf{u}}
and
{\mathbf{v}}
, we have
\res_{u_{1}=v_{k}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})=\res_{u_{2}=v_{k}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})=\res_{u_{1}=v_{1}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})
, and
\res_{u_{1}=v_{k_{1}},u_{2}=v_{k_{2}}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})=\res_{u_{1}=v_{1},u_{2}=v_{2}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})
whenever
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
.
Therefore, the two middle sums are equal, and the double sum runs over all pairs of distinct indices. Thus, collecting terms and using the symmetry, the right-hand side is equal to

\begin{split}\int_{\Gamma_{3}^{2}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{u}}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\,\,F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})+2n(-2\pi\mathrm{i})\int_{\Gamma_{3}}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\res_{u_{1}=v_{1}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}})+2\binom{n}{2}(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\res_{u_{1}=v_{1},u_{2}=v_{2}}F({\mathbf{u}},{\mathbf{v}}),\end{split}

which coincides with the right side of (
7.65
) when
m=2
and
n\geq 2
.
∎

Corollary 7.22
.

Let
g
and
h
be analytic functions in a region
\Omega
.
Suppose
\Gamma_{1}
,
\Gamma_{2}
, and
\Gamma_{3}
are nested Jordan curves in
\Omega
that can be continuously deformed into one another within
\Omega
.
Let
m,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
a,b,c
be integers such that
a\geq m
,
b\geq m\vee n
, and
c\geq n
.
For all vectors
\mathbf{r}_{1}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{a}
,
(\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{s}_{2})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{a-m}
,
(\mathbf{s}_{3},\mathbf{s}_{4})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{b-m}
,
(\mathbf{s}_{5},\mathbf{s}_{6})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{b-n}
,
(\mathbf{s}_{7},\mathbf{s}_{8})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{c-n}
, and
\mathbf{r}_{2}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{c}
, we have

\begin{split}&\int_{\Gamma_{1}^{m}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{u}}\int_{\Gamma_{3}^{n}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\,\,\mathrm{K}_{a}(\mathbf{r}_{1}|\mathbf{s}_{1},{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{s}_{2})\mathrm{K}_{b}(\mathbf{s}_{3},{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{s}_{4}|\mathbf{s}_{5},{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{s}_{6})\mathrm{K}_{c}(\mathbf{s}_{7},{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{s}_{8}|\mathbf{r}_{2})\prod_{p=1}^{m}g(u_{p})\prod_{q=1}^{n}h(v_{q})\\
&=\sum_{i=0}^{m\wedge n}(-1)^{\#}(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{i}i!\binom{m}{i}\binom{n}{i}R_{i},\end{split}

(7.66)

where
\#
denotes an integer whose precise value is not specified here
10
10

10

If
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Gamma_{3}
are nested are nested such that
\Gamma_{1}
is the innermost curve and
\Gamma_{3}
is the outermost, then
\#=i(1+\mathsf{d}(\mathbf{s}_{3})+\mathsf{d}(\mathbf{s}_{5}))
, where
\mathsf{d}(\mathbf{s})=d
for vectors
\mathbf{s}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{d}
. If
\Gamma_{1}
is the outermost and
\Gamma_{3}
is the innermost, then
\#=i(\mathsf{d}(\mathbf{s}_{3})+\mathsf{d}(\mathbf{s}_{5}))
.
and

\begin{split}R_{i}&=\int_{\Gamma_{1}^{n-i}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{v}}\int_{\Gamma_{2}^{i}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{w}\int_{\Gamma_{3}^{m-i}}\mathrm{d}{\mathbf{u}}\,\,\mathrm{K}_{a}(\mathbf{r}_{1}|\mathbf{s}_{1},\mathbf{w},{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{s}_{2})\mathrm{K}_{b-i}(\mathbf{s}_{3},{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{s}_{4}|\mathbf{s}_{5},{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{s}_{6})\mathrm{K}_{c}(\mathbf{s}_{7},\mathbf{w},{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{s}_{8}|\mathbf{r}_{2})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\prod_{p=1}^{m-i}g(u_{p})\prod_{q=1}^{n-i}h(v_{q})\prod_{r=1}^{i}g(w_{r})h(w_{r}).\end{split}

(7.67)

Proof.

Assume that
\Gamma_{1}
is the innermost curve and
\Gamma_{3}
is the outermost; the proof is similar if the nesting order is reversed.
From the Cauchy determinant formula, the integrand on the left side of (
7.66
) satisfies the conditions of Lemma
7.21
. We now compute the residues.
The Cauchy determinant formula also implies that

\res_{u_{1}=v_{1},\cdots,u_{i}=v_{i}}\mathrm{K}_{b}(\mathbf{s}_{3},{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{s}_{4}|\mathbf{s}_{5},{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{s}_{6})=\pm\mathrm{K}_{b-i}(\mathbf{s}_{3},\hat{\mathbf{u}},\mathbf{s}_{4}|\mathbf{s}_{5},\hat{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{s}_{6})

where
\hat{\mathbf{u}}
denotes the vector
{\mathbf{u}}
with entries
u_{1},\cdots,u_{i}
removed and
\hat{\mathbf{v}}
denotes
{\mathbf{v}}
with entries
v_{1},\cdots,v_{i}
removed. The sign is not specified, as it is not relevant for our purposes.
Thus, the result follows from Lemma
7.21
, after setting the variables

(v_{1},\cdots,v_{i})=\mathbf{w},\quad(u_{i+1},\cdots,u_{m})={\mathbf{u}},\quad(v_{i+1},\cdots,v_{n})={\mathbf{v}}.

∎

Corollary 7.23
.

Define

\mathsf{A}^{m,n}_{i}:=i!\binom{m}{i}\binom{n}{i}.

(7.68)

1.

If
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\cdots\alpha^{m}\beta^{n}\cdots
with
\{\alpha,\beta\}\notin\{\{1,2\},\{2,3\},\{12,3\},\{1,23\}\}
, then
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{\prime}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
where

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\cdots\beta^{m}\alpha^{n}\cdots
.

2.

If
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\cdots\alpha^{m}\beta^{n}\cdots
with
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in\{\{1,2\},\{2,3\},\{12,3\},\{1,23\}\}
, then
11
11

11

For example, if
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=\cdots 3^{m}(12)^{n}\cdots
, then
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}=\cdots(12)^{n-i}(123)^{i}3^{m-i}\cdots
.

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{m\wedge n}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}^{m,n}_{i}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}=\cdots\beta^{n-i}(\alpha\beta)^{i}\alpha^{m-i}\cdots.\end{split}

3.

If
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=\cdots\alpha^{m}\beta^{n}\cdots
with
\{\alpha,\beta\}\notin\{\{1,2\},\{2,3\},\{12,3\},\{1,23\}\}
, then

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{\prime}}
where
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{\prime}=\cdots\beta^{m}\alpha^{n}\cdots
.

4.

If
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=\cdots\alpha^{m}\beta^{n}\cdots
with
\{\alpha,\beta\}\in\{\{1,2\},\{2,3\},\{12,3\},\{1,23\}\}
, then

\begin{split}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{m\wedge n}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}^{m,n}_{i}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}=\cdots\beta^{n-i}(\alpha\beta)^{i}\alpha^{m-i}\cdots.\end{split}

Proof.

(a) From the formula (
7.22
),
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
as a function of
\boldsymbol{\xi}
can have a pole at
\xi^{\alpha}_{i}=\xi^{\beta}_{j}
only when
\{\alpha,\beta\}=\{1,2\},\{2,3\},\{12,3\}
, or
\{1,23\}
.
Therefore, (a) holds because
\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
is analytic at
\xi^{\alpha}_{i}=\xi^{\beta}_{j}
for all
i,j
when
{\alpha,\beta}
does not belong to these sets.

(b) From (
7.24
),

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{|{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|+|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}|}}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\eta}^{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}\,\Pi^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}){\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}).

Fixing the
\boldsymbol{\eta}
-variables, we apply Corollary
7.22

with
{\mathbf{u}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\alpha}
,
{\mathbf{v}}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{\beta}
, and set
\mathbf{w}=\boldsymbol{\xi}^{(\alpha\beta)}
.
Since

f_{L,\alpha}(z)f_{L,\beta}(z)=f_{L,(\alpha\beta)}(z)
, we see that
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
becomes
{\mathsf{F}}_{L}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}|{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}(\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})
upon computing
R_{i}
in (
7.67
).
Thus, the result follows.

The arguments for (c) and (d) are similar.
∎

We now express the integrals in Proposition
7.13
as sums of integrals to which Proposition
7.11
applies.
In doing so, we obtain the following two results, which are applicable to a broader class of integrals.

Lemma 7.24
.

Let
\mathbf{n}=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
and
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}1^{n_{1}}2^{n_{22}}3^{n_{32}}
with
n_{21}+n_{22}=n_{2}
and
n_{31}+n_{32}=n_{3}
.
Then the following hold for every
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
. Here,
\#
denotes an integer whose exact value is not specified.
12
12

12

It is possible to determine the value of
\#
. For example,
\#=i(1+b_{1})+j+k(i-j)+(j+k)(b_{3}+n_{2}-n_{3})
for (a), if
\type({\boldsymbol{\tau}})=(b_{123},b_{12},b_{23},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3})
.

1.

We have

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}\sum_{j=0}^{i\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge n_{31}}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}^{n_{1},n_{22}}_{i}\mathsf{A}^{i,n_{32}}_{j}\mathsf{A}^{n_{2}-i,n_{31}}_{k}\,\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau},

where
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=2^{n_{2}-i-k}(23)^{k}3^{n_{3}-j-k}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i}
.

2.

We have

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}\sum_{j=0}^{i\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge(n_{32}-j)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}^{n_{1},n_{22}}_{i}\mathsf{A}^{i,n_{32}}_{j}\mathsf{A}^{n_{2}-i,n_{32}-j}_{k}\,\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau},

where
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=3^{n_{3}-j-k}(23)^{k}2^{n_{2}-i-k}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i}
.

3.

We have

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{22}\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{j=0}^{n_{21}\wedge(n_{32}-i)}\sum_{k=0}^{n_{1}\wedge i}\sum_{l=0}^{(n_{21}-j)\wedge(n_{1}-k)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}^{n_{22},n_{32}}_{i}\mathsf{A}^{n_{21},n_{32}-i}_{j}\mathsf{A}^{n_{1},i}_{k}\mathsf{A}^{n_{21}-j,n_{1}-k}_{l}\,\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}}_{\tau},

where
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{i+j-k}(123)^{k}1^{n_{1}-k-l}(12)^{l}2^{n_{2}-i-j-l}
.

Proof.

We repeatedly use Corollary
7.23
to yield the following.

(a) First,

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}(-1)^{\#_{i}}\mathsf{A}^{n_{1},n_{22}}_{i}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}2^{n_{22}-i}(12)^{i}1^{n_{1}-i}3^{n_{32}}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{2}-i}(12)^{i}1^{n_{1}-i}3^{n_{32}}.

(7.69)

Moreover,
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\mathrm{I}^{\hat{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
where
\hat{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{i}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{2}-i}(12)^{i}3^{n_{32}}1^{n_{1}-i}
. We further obtain

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\mathrm{I}^{\hat{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{i}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{j=0}^{i\wedge n_{32}}(-1)^{\#_{j}}\mathsf{A}_{j}^{i,n_{32}}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}}_{\tau},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{2}-i}3^{n_{32}-j}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i},

(7.70)

where

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge n_{31}}(-1)^{\#_{k}}\mathsf{A}_{k}^{n_{2}-i,n_{31}},\quad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=2^{n_{2}-i-k}(23)^{k}3^{n_{3}-j-k}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i}.

(7.71)

Thus, we obtain (a).

(b) Instead of (
7.71
), we write

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge(n_{32}-j)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{k}^{n_{2}-i,n_{32}-j}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau},\quad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=3^{n_{31}}3^{n_{32}-j-k}(23)^{k}2^{n_{2}-i-k}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i}.

(c) We find that

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{22}\wedge n_{32}}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{i}^{n_{22},n_{32}}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{\tau},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}1^{n_{1}}3^{n_{32}-i}(23)^{i}2^{n_{22}-i}

and
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{\tau}=\mathrm{I}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{\tau}
where
\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}3^{n_{32}-i}1^{n_{1}}(23)^{i}2^{n_{22}-i}
. We further find that

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{\tau}=\mathrm{I}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{i}}_{\tau}=\sum_{j=0}^{n_{21}\wedge(n_{32}-i)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{j}^{n_{21},n_{32}-i}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}}_{\tau},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{j}2^{n_{21}-j}1^{n_{1}}(23)^{i}2^{n_{22}-i}.

Additionally,

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ij}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{n_{1}\wedge i}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{k}^{n_{1},i}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{j}2^{n_{21}-j}(23)^{i-k}(123)^{k}1^{n_{1}-k}2^{n_{22}-i}

where
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau}=\mathrm{I}^{\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau}
, and

\hat{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{i+j-k}(123)^{k}2^{n_{21}-j}1^{n_{1}-k}2^{n_{22}-i}
.
Finally,

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{l=0}^{(n_{21}-j)\wedge(n_{1}-k)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{l}^{n_{21}-j,n_{1}-k}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}}_{\tau},\quad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{i+j-k}(123)^{k}1^{n_{1}-k-l}(12)^{l}2^{n_{2}-i-j-l}.

∎

Lemma 7.25
.

Let
\mathbf{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{3}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{21}}1^{n_{1}}2^{n_{22}}3^{n_{32}}
, where
n_{21}+n_{22}=n_{2}
and
n_{31}+n_{32}=n_{3}
.
Then, for every
{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\in\mathcal{S}_{\mathbf{n}}
,

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}\sum_{j=0}^{n_{31}\wedge(n_{2}-i)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}^{n_{1},n_{22}}_{i}\mathsf{A}^{n_{2}-i,n_{31}}_{j}\,\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{ij}}

for some
\#\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{ij}=2^{n_{2}-i-j}(23)^{j}3^{n_{31}-j}(12)^{i}1^{n_{1}-i}3^{n_{32}}
.

Proof.

Corollary
7.23
implies

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{i}^{n_{1},n_{22}}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}=3^{n_{31}}2^{n_{2}-i}(12)^{i}1^{n_{1}-i}3^{n_{32}},

and that

\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{i}}=\sum_{j=0}^{n_{31}\wedge(n_{2}-i)}(-1)^{\#}\mathsf{A}_{j}^{n_{31},n_{2}-i}\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{ij}},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{ij}=2^{n_{2}-i-j}(23)^{j}3^{n_{31}-j}(12)^{i}1^{n_{1}-i}3^{n_{32}}.

∎

The above lemmas yield the proofs of Lemma
7.8
and
7.9
.

Proofs of Lemmas
7.8
and
7.9
.

These results follow from Lemmas
7.24
and
7.25
upon careful tracking of the signs
(-1)^{\#}
.
It is straightforward to verify the signs explicitly, and the results follow.
∎

We now focus on the proof of Proposition
7.13
.
The proof uses the following estimates.

Lemma 7.26
.

The following estimates hold for every
n,n^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}
:

1.

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a\leq n\wedge n^{\prime}}a!\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-a)!}\leq\frac{2^{2(n+n^{\prime})}n!n^{\prime}!}{\sqrt{(n\vee n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime})!}}
.

2.

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a+b\leq n\wedge n^{\prime}}a!b!\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-a-b)!}\leq\frac{2^{3(n+n^{\prime})}n!n^{\prime}!}{\sqrt{(n\vee n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime})!}}
.

3.

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq a+b,c\leq n\wedge n^{\prime}}a!b!c!\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-a-b-c)!}\leq\frac{2^{4(n+n^{\prime})}n!n^{\prime}!}{\sqrt{(n\vee n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime})!}}
.

Proof.

Since the multinomial coefficients
\frac{k!}{a!(k-a)!},\frac{k!}{a!b!(k-a-b)!},\frac{k!}{a!b!c!(k-a-b-c)!}
are each at least
1
, we find that

\begin{split}&(a)=\sum_{0\leq a\leq n\wedge n^{\prime}}\frac{a!(n+n^{\prime}-a)!}{\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-a)!}}\leq\frac{(n+n^{\prime})!}{\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime})!}}(n\wedge n^{\prime}+1)\\
&(b)=\sum_{0\leq a+b\leq n\wedge n^{\prime}}\frac{a!b!(n+n^{\prime}-a-b)!}{\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-a-b)!}}\leq\frac{(n+n^{\prime})!}{\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime})!}}(n\wedge n^{\prime}+1)^{2}\\
&(c)=\sum_{0\leq a+b,c\leq n\wedge n^{\prime}}\frac{a!b!c!(n+n^{\prime}-a-b-b)!}{\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-a-b-c)!}}\leq\frac{(n+n^{\prime})!}{\sqrt{(n+n^{\prime}-2(n\wedge n^{\prime}))!}}(n\wedge n^{\prime}+1)^{3}.\end{split}

Since
\binom{n+n^{\prime}}{n}\leq 2^{n+n^{\prime}}
, it follows that
(n+n^{\prime})!\leq 2^{n+n^{\prime}}n!n^{\prime}!
.
Additionally,
n\wedge n^{\prime}+1\leq n+n^{\prime}\leq 2^{n+n^{\prime}}
and

n+n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime}\geq n\vee n^{\prime}-n\wedge n^{\prime}
. Putting these together we obtain the result.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
7.13
.

Proof of Proposition
7.13
.

We use Lemma
7.24
, Lemma
7.25
, Proposition
7.11
,
and Lemma
7.26
.
Let
L_{0},C
, and
c
be the positive constants appearing in Proposition
7.11
.
Note that
\mathsf{A}^{m,n}_{i}\leq 2^{m+n}i!
.

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{1}\cup\mathsf{R}_{2}
.
From Lemma
7.24
(a), we have

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq 2^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}\sum_{j=0}^{i\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge n_{31}}i!j!k!\,|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau}|,\quad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=2^{n_{2}-i-k}(23)^{k}3^{n_{3}-j-k}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i}.

If
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk})=(j,i-j,k,n_{1}-i,n_{2}-i-k,n_{3}-j-k)=(1,0,0,0,0,0)
, then it necessarily follows that
n_{1}=n_{2}=n_{3}=1
(and
i=j=1
and
k=0
), which contradicts the assumption that
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1)
.
Hence, Proposition
7.11
(a) applies to each
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau}
, and we find that

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq(2^{2}C)^{|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\sqrt{n_{1}!n_{3}!}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}\sum_{j=0}^{i\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge n_{31}}i!j!k!\sqrt{(n_{1}+n_{2}-i)!(n_{2}+n_{3}-j-k)!}.

Note that
j+k\leq i+(n_{2}-i)=n_{2}
and
j+k\leq n_{32}+n_{31}=n_{3}
. Hence, the triple sum is bounded by

\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{2}}\sum_{0\leq j+k\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}}i!j!k!\sqrt{(n_{1}+n_{2}-i)!(n_{2}+n_{3}-j-k)!}.

Applying Lemma
7.26
(a) and (b), and possibly adjusting the value of the constant
C
, we obtain the desired result (
7.45
).

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{3}\cup\mathsf{R}_{4}
. From Lemma
7.24
(b), we have

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq 2^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{22}}\sum_{j=0}^{i\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{k=0}^{(n_{2}-i)\wedge(n_{32}-j)}i!j!k!\,|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau}|,\quad{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}=3^{n_{3}-j-k}(23)^{k}2^{n_{2}-i-k}(123)^{j}(12)^{i-j}1^{n_{1}-i}.

Again,
\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk})=(j,i-j,k,n_{1}-i,n_{2}-i-k,n_{3}-j-k)
is not equal to
(1,0,0,0,0,0)
, and thus Proposition
7.11
(b) applies to each
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijk}}_{\tau}
.
Noting that
j+k\leq i+(n_{2}-i)=n_{2}
and
j+k\leq j+(n_{32}-j)\leq n_{3}
, we find that

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq(2^{2}C)^{|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\sqrt{n_{1}!n_{3}!}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{2}}\sum_{0\leq j+k\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}}i!j!k!\sqrt{(n_{1}+n_{2}-i)!(n_{2}+n_{3}-j-k)!}.

The result(
7.45
) then follows from Lemma
7.26
(a) and (b).

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{5}
. From Lemma
7.24
(c), we have

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq 2^{2|\mathbf{n}|}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{22}\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{j=0}^{n_{21}\wedge(n_{32}-i)}\sum_{k=0}^{n_{1}\wedge i}\sum_{l=0}^{(n_{21}-j)\wedge(n_{1}-k)}i!j!k!l!\,|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}}_{\tau}|

where

{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{i+j-k}(123)^{k}1^{n_{1}-k-l}(12)^{l}2^{n_{2}-i-j-l}.

We again see that
\mathbf{a}:=\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl})=(k,l,i+j-k,n_{1}-k-l,n_{2}-i-j-l,n_{3}-i-j)
is not
(1,0,0,0,0,0)
since
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1)
.
Moreover,
a_{123}+a_{1}=k+(n_{1}-k-l)=n_{1}-l\geq n_{1}-(n_{21}-j)\geq n_{1}-n_{21}\geq 1
where the final inequality uses the assumption
n_{1}\geq n_{21}+1
.
Hence, Proposition
7.11
(c) applies to
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}}_{\tau}
.
Noting that
i+j\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}
and
k+l\leq n_{1}\wedge n_{2}
,

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq(2^{2}C)^{|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\sqrt{n_{1}!n_{3}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j=0\\
i+j\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}\end{subarray}}^{n_{2}\wedge n_{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l=0\\
k+l\leq n_{1}\wedge n_{2}\end{subarray}}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{2}}i!j!k!l!\sqrt{(n_{1}+n_{2}-k-l)!(n_{2}+n_{3}-i-j)!}.

We obtain the result (
7.45
) from Lemma
7.26
(b).

Suppose
\mathsf{q}\in\mathsf{R}_{6}\cup\mathsf{R}_{7}
.
From Lemma
7.24
(c) and Lemma
7.25
, we have

\begin{split}|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq&2^{4|\mathbf{n}|}\sum_{i=0}^{n_{22}\wedge n_{32}}\sum_{j=0}^{n_{21}\wedge(n_{32}-i)}\sum_{k=0}^{n_{1}\wedge i}\sum_{l=0}^{(n_{21}-j)\wedge(n_{1}-k)}\sum_{p=0}^{n_{1}\wedge n^{\prime}_{22}}\sum_{q=0}^{(n_{2}-p)\wedge n^{\prime}_{31}}i!j!k!l!p!q!\,|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}}_{\tau_{pq}}|\end{split}

where

\begin{split}&{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}=3^{n_{3}-i-j}(23)^{i+j-k}(123)^{k}1^{n_{1}-k-l}(12)^{l}2^{n_{2}-i-j-l},\qquad{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{pq}=2^{n_{2}-p-q}(23)^{q}3^{n^{\prime}_{31}-q}(12)^{p}1^{n_{1}-p}3^{n^{\prime}_{32}}.\end{split}

Let
\mathbf{a}=\type({\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl})
and
\mathbf{b}=\type({\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{pq})
.
If
(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},b_{2},a_{3})=(k,l,i+j-k,n_{1}-k-l,n_{2}-p-q,n_{3}-i-j)=(1,0,0,0,0,0)
,
then
n_{1}=n_{3}=1
,
n_{2}=p+q
.
Since
b_{1}=n_{1}-p\geq 0
and
b_{3}=n^{\prime}_{31}-q\geq 0
, we find that

n_{2}=p+q\leq n_{1}+n^{\prime}_{31}\leq n_{1}+n_{3}=2
.
Given our assumption that
\mathbf{n}\neq(1,1,1),(1,2,1)
, we see that
(a_{123},a_{12},a_{23},a_{1},b_{2},a_{3})\neq(1,0,0,0,0,0)
.
Furthermore,
a_{123}+a_{1}=n_{1}-l\geq n_{1}-n_{21}+j\geq n_{1}-n_{21}\geq 1
by assumption.
Hence, Proposition
7.11
(d) applies to each
\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{ijkl}}_{\tau_{pq}}
.
Noting that
i+j\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}
,
k+l\leq n_{1}\wedge n_{2}
,
p\leq n_{1}\wedge n_{2}
, and
q\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}
, we find

|\mathrm{I}^{{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}|\leq(2^{4}C)^{|\mathbf{n}|}e^{-cL}\mathsf{Z}_{L}\sqrt{n_{1}!n_{3}!}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j,q=0\\
i+j\leq n_{2}\wedge n_{3}\end{subarray}}^{n_{2}\wedge n_{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,l,p=0\\
k+l\leq n_{1}\wedge n_{2}\end{subarray}}^{n_{1}\wedge n_{2}}i!j!k!l!p!q!\sqrt{(n_{1}+n_{2}-k-l-p)!(n_{2}+n_{3}-i-j-q)!}

Applying Lemma
7.26
(c), we obtain the result (
7.45
).
∎
